### Language switch

- **Entry point:** Two controls that both call `setLocale` in the i18n store ([frontend/src/i18n/useI18nStore.ts](../frontend/src/i18n/useI18nStore.ts)): the toolbar toggle `main-lang-toggle` ([frontend/src/components/Toolbar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/Toolbar.tsx)), labelled `EN` when English is active and `中` when 繁體中文 is; and the menu bar's **View > Language** items `menu-view-lang-en` ("English") and `menu-view-lang-zh` ("中文 (繁體)") ([frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx)).
- **Trigger:** A click on the toggle (it cycles `en` ↔ `zh_TW`), or picking a locale under View > Language.
- **Behaviour:** `setLocale(code)` persists the choice with `PATCH /api/settings` (`ui.locale`, written to settings.json), then fetches `GET /api/i18n/{code}` and swaps in the new catalog; every label rendered through `useT()` / `t()` re-renders at once, with no page reload and no confirm dialog. If the persist fails, the failure is logged (`console.warn`) and the switch lasts for this session only; if the catalog fetch fails, the locale code still flips with an empty catalog, so the English fallbacks render. On page load `initI18n()` reads `ui.locale` from `GET /api/settings` and fetches that catalog, so the choice survives a reload and a relaunch. The manifest and result tree live in a separate Zustand store that a locale switch never touches. The retired desktop client rebuilt its whole window on a switch, which is why it needed a confirm prompt and the [#428](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/428) manifest-reload fix. The web needs neither, because React never destroys and recreates the component tree on a switch. The View > Language menu is a checkmark picker: the active locale's item carries a trailing `✓` (`aria-hidden`) and the other carries none, so both options stay visible. Picking the already-active locale still round-trips through `setLocale` (there is no no-op short-circuit).
- **Toolbar text in each locale:**
  - `main-scan-button`: `Scan` (en) / `掃描` (zh_TW)
  - `main-execute-button`: `Execute` (en) / `執行` (zh_TW)
  - `main-settings-button`: `Settings` (en) / `設定` (zh_TW)
  - `main-manifest-open-button`: `Open` (en) / `開啟` (zh_TW)
- **Conditions / variants:** The UI offers exactly the two shipped locales, `en` and `zh_TW`, hard-coded in both controls. The backend discovers locales from the `translations/<code>.yml` files and lists them in the `available` field of `GET /api/i18n/{locale}` (an unknown code gets a 404), but the frontend does not read that list, so adding a third locale takes a new YAML file **and** a UI change. `GET /api/i18n/{locale}` serves only the `web.*` keys. The desktop-era namespaces that remain in `en.yml` are never sent to the browser.
- **Coverage gap closed (web audit fixes 5+6, [#669](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/669)):** a UI/UX sweep found the toggle worked but a whole class of components never called `useT()`, so switching to 中文(繁體) left them in English — notably both right-click context menus (main tree + Execute dialog), LockConfirmDialog, PruneConfirmDialog, RemoveFromListConfirmDialog, SettingsDialog, ExecuteDialog's own chrome, FullResViewer, PreviewPane (incl. its metadata labels), the `similarityLabel`/"Ref" formatter, FileRow's "Ref" badge, and two of DeleteConfirmDialog's three branches. All now route through `useT()`/`t('web.*', …)`; `tests/test_web_i18n_callsite_keys.py` pins every callsite key against `en.yml`, and both catalogs carry the identical `web.*` key set (verified — no asymmetry; **303 keys** as of the 2026-09-14 copy audit, **362** on 2026-09-29 and still symmetric).
- **Second coverage sweep (copy audit batch 1, 2026-09-14):** an owner trial found the same class of leak in surfaces the first sweep missed. Now catalog-driven: the whole **Scan dialog** including its running-state panel and source rows (the largest remaining English surface — see [Web — ScanDialog source/output persistence](#web--scandialog-sourceoutput-persistence)), the result tree's **group header row** ("Group 3 · 5 files" stayed English in a zh_TW session — `web.tree.*` is the first mirror of the desktop-era `tree.*` namespace), the Execute dialog's two **warning banners** and its preview `alt`, the near-duplicate similarity label (`~` → `~dup`), the **status-bar error lines** (which rendered raw backend developer text — see [Main window — status bar baseline](#main-window--status-bar-baseline)), and the accessible names on the menu bar, the lock padlock and the inline decision control. Pluralisation was split per count in four places that read "1 groups" / "1 files" / "1 files found" / "row(s)". zh_TW punctuation was normalised to full-width in the Set-Action labels and the scan parity note. **Copy audit batch 2 (2026-09-18)** then settled both of the questions batch 1 had deferred. The three decision labels became the ONE site-wide vocabulary **Keep / Delete / Skip** (保留 / 刪除 / 略過) for the wire values `""` / `"delete"` / `"ignore"` — replacing four different triples (tree control `None / Delete / Ignore`, right-click `Keep (clear decision) / Delete / Remove from list`, Execute filter `All decided / Delete only / Remove only`, Execute badge `Delete / Remove`), with short forms on controls and long forms in menus and tooltips; every user-facing "delete" long form names the Recycle Bin and the Skip long form names the file's fate on disk first. The word "Remove"／「移除」 no longer renders as a decision label anywhere. **The two IMMEDIATE actions are worded apart from the staged one** (review round 2): the row control STAGES a reversible `user_decision='ignore'`, while the result-tree right-click item and the List menu FINALIZE `outcome='ignored'` with no confirm and no undo — so those two read **"Skip now — drop from this review"**／「立即略過 — 從本次檢閱中移除」 and **"Skip Selected now"**／「立即略過選取項目」 (`web.context_menu.skip_now`, `web.menu.list_remove`). One word for both would make the reversible and the irreversible action look identical. The **Action** column renders the classifier value through `web.classification.*` instead of the raw enum (`EXACT` → "Exact copy" / 「完全相同」) — and **layout slice C then removed that column outright** (open questions Q1): the classification never varies independently of the similarity badge beside it, so **Action** now heads the decision control and the classification moved to the badge's `title` tooltip, in the same `web.classification.*` words. See [Web — Daylight theme and results-tree framing](#web--daylight-theme-and-results-tree-framing) § Columns.
- **Related:** Originally [PR #157](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/157); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s22_language_switch.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s22_language_switch.py) (live switch + persistence across reload + bilingual copy probe) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s58_language_switch_preserves_manifest.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s58_language_switch_preserves_manifest.py) (#428 regression guard — result-tree rows unchanged across a switch); i18n store `frontend/src/i18n/useI18nStore.ts`; backend catalog `GET /api/i18n/{locale}` (`app/web/routes/i18n.py`); persistence `PATCH /api/settings` (`app/web/routes/settings.py`); callsite-key pin `tests/test_web_i18n_callsite_keys.py`; translator workflow in [`docs/i18n.md`](i18n.md).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client; `web.*` key sets re-counted, en 362 / zh_TW 362, no asymmetry); 2026-09-14 (copy audit batch 1 — second coverage sweep, s22 extended with a bilingual copy probe); 2026-06-22 (web lang toggle functional, scenarios s22+s58 ported)

### List menu — Remove from List

- **Entry point:** Menu bar **List > Skip Selected now** ([frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx)), handled by `handleRemoveFromListSelected` in [frontend/src/App.tsx](../frontend/src/App.tsx).
- **Trigger:** User selects one or more file rows in the result tree and picks **List > Skip Selected now**.
- **Behaviour:** Calls `store.removeFromList(selectedPaths)` → `POST /api/remove` → `remove_from_review` ([core/app_service/execute_service.py](../core/app_service/execute_service.py)), which finalizes `outcome='ignored'` for those rows (the files stay on disk) and returns the reloaded review groups. The store replaces the tree's groups with them and clears the selection, so the rows drop out of the view at once — no confirm, no save step. If the remove leaves a group with a single member, the store then runs the singleton-prune offer (see [Singleton-prune offer after destructive ops (#426)](#singleton-prune-offer-after-destructive-ops-426)). This is the immediate path; it is distinct from the staged, reversible **Skip** decision set by the row control or by [Bulk regex — remove from list (deferred decision)](#bulk-regex--remove-from-list-deferred-decision).
- **Conditions / variants:** Works with single or multi-select. Lock-aware: the server re-reads each row's lock state from the manifest and answers 409 `locked_paths` if any target is locked; the store then opens the lock-confirm dialog, whose force option unlocks those rows and removes them all, and whose **Unlocked Only** option re-runs the remove without the locked rows (see [Execute Action — lock-confirm dialog](#execute-action--lock-confirm-dialog)). The route rejects a path outside the allowed roots before any write. The result-tree right-click **Skip now — drop from this review** item and the Execute dialog's remove (behind its own confirm) call the same `store.removeFromList`.
- **Web parity (#678-D):** the web MenuBar carries the same **List → Skip Selected now** entry (labelled "Remove from List" until the 2026-09-18 vocabulary decision; it finalizes `outcome='ignored'` on the spot, so it carries the immediacy marker the reversible row control does not) (`menu-list` / `menu-list-remove-from-list`, [MenuBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx)), wired to the SAME `store.removeFromList` path as the web context menu — finalizes `outcome='ignored'` immediately (the web has no dirty-flag/save step), lock-aware via the standard 409 flow. Web variant: with no selection the item is **disabled** (same gating idiom as Execute (only selected)) instead of Qt's "No items selected" QMessageBox; there is no "Removed N items" status-bar echo.
- **Web undo (#909):** both immediate Skip entry points — the result-tree context menu's **Skip now — drop from this review** (file and group variants) and **List → Skip Selected now** — still finalize with no confirm, but a successful skip now raises the single-slot undo toast (`main-toast`, the same slot as keep-best): *"N files dropped from this review · Undo"* / 「已從本次檢閱中移除 N 個檔案 · 復原」 (`web.toast.skip_now`), ~6 s, paused while hovered. **Undo** calls `POST /api/restore`, which returns exactly those rows from `outcome='ignored'` to `outcome=''` — a skip writes nothing else (no file I/O, no delete CSV, no decision or lock change), so the skipped rows come back with their staged decisions. If the singleton prune that follows a skip took the skipped row's partner (pref **always**, or **Remove** in the prune dialog while the toast is still up), Undo restores the partner too — otherwise the restored row would be a one-member group the tree never shows; a prune is only ever folded into the toast of the skip that started it. **One thing Undo does not put back:** a lock. If that partner was a **locked** singleton, the prune's lock dialog had to unlock it first (**Unlock & Apply**, its own confirmed step); Undo returns the partner to the review **unlocked**. The restore never touches a `deleted` row. Loading another manifest (Open, or a scan that auto-loads) dismisses the toast — its rows live in the manifest that was loaded when it was raised. Not covered: the Execute dialog's Skip (already behind its own confirm, unchanged), and a skip that 409s on a locked row (nothing is written; the lock dialog's retry — including Unlock & Apply — raises no toast).
- **Related:** Originally [PR #158](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/158); web menu entry #678-D; undo toast: [#909](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/909) (`frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts` `removeFromList(…, { undoable })` / `undoSkipNow`, `core/app_service/execute_service.py::restore_to_review`). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s20_multi_remove_from_list.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s20_multi_remove_from_list.py) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s21_list_menu_remove.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s21_list_menu_remove.py); unit tests `frontend/src/store/useAppStore.test.ts` (`removeFromList` success + 409 paths), `frontend/src/components/MenuBar.test.tsx`, `tests/test_web_execute_service.py` (`remove_from_review`, and `TestRestoreToReview` for the undo).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-29 (#909 undo toast — store/Toast/service unit tests; s20 branch D drives Undo, s21 reads the List-menu toast, both in CI `web-scenario-batch`); 2026-07-11 (web menu entry, #678-D)

### Log menu

- **Entry point:** None — removed with the desktop client in #646. The web menu bar has File / Action / List / View only ([frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx)), and the web API has no log endpoint.
- **Trigger:** None.
- **Behaviour:** The retired desktop client's **Log** menu opened the latest app log, the latest delete log, or either log directory in the OS default application. The web offers no way to open them. The shared backend still writes both kinds of file, so they can be opened by hand. Every launch through `launcher.py` (the app's only entry point) calls `infrastructure.logging.init_logging()`, which writes a rotating loguru log `app_YYYYMMDD.log` (INFO level, 10 MB rotation, 10-day retention, zip-compressed) under `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Local\PhotoManager\logs`. Every web **Execute** run that attempts at least one delete writes an audit CSV `delete_<YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS>.csv` under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\PhotoManager\delete_logs`, with the columns `GroupNumber, FilePath, Success, Reason`, through the shared `write_delete_log` (called from `execute_decisions` in [core/app_service/execute_service.py](../core/app_service/execute_service.py)). `POST /api/execute` returns that CSV's path as `log_path`, but no web UI shows it.
- **Conditions / variants:** The web CSV records every successful delete, every failed delete (including a path refused as `outside_allowed_roots`), and every file that left the disk but whose `outcome` write failed (recorded as success, reason `outcome write failed`). Its `GroupNumber` is always `0`. Files already missing at execute time and rows finalized as Skip (`outcome='ignored'`) are not recorded, and a run with no delete attempt writes no CSV. A failed CSV write is logged and leaves `log_path` `null`; the execute itself still succeeds. No test pins the web execute path's CSV; `tests/test_delete_service.py` covers `write_delete_log` through `DeleteService`. Exposing the logs through the browser would need a product decision plus new endpoints and a menu.
- **Related:** QA scenario `s18_log_menu` — `status: skip` in [qa/web/scenario_map.yml](../qa/web/scenario_map.yml) (no web equivalent surface). Delete-audit CSV for the UI delete path: #505. Log helpers in `infrastructure/logging.py`; launcher start-up in [launcher.py](../launcher.py) (see [Web — desktop shell (pywebview launcher)](#web--desktop-shell-pywebview-launcher)).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client; log locations and CSV contents code-read, not live-run)

### Main window — close-during-scan confirm

- **Entry point:** `useBeforeUnloadGuard` ([frontend/src/hooks/useBeforeUnloadGuard.ts](../frontend/src/hooks/useBeforeUnloadGuard.ts)), mounted once in `App`; the backend's cooperative cancel `POST /api/scan/{id}/cancel` ([app/web/routes/scan.py](../app/web/routes/scan.py)) and the app's shutdown lifespan ([app/web/main.py](../app/web/main.py)).
- **Trigger:** The user reloads, navigates away from or closes the page while `scan.status === "running"`; or closes the desktop-shell window while a scan runs.
- **Behaviour:** The browser gives a page no close event it can answer with its own Yes/No dialog, so the guard has two parts, both keyed on the live `scan.status` read via `getState()` when the event fires. (1) **Warn:** a `beforeunload` handler arms the browser's native Leave/Stay confirm (`preventDefault()` + `returnValue = ""`) only while a scan is running; idle / finished / failed / cancelled pages navigate freely. The browser owns the prompt's wording and buttons. (2) **Cancel on Leave ([#703](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/703)):** on a confirmed unload a `pagehide` handler sends a best-effort `navigator.sendBeacon('/api/scan/{id}/cancel')`, so the scan does not keep running with nobody watching it. The handler listens on `pagehide`, not on `beforeunload`, which fires before the user chooses and would cancel even on Stay, and not on `visibilitychange`, which fires on a tab switch. A bfcache stash (`e.persisted`) is skipped, and the call is optional-chained so the handler never throws where `sendBeacon` is missing. Closing the desktop-shell window shuts the embedded server down, and the lifespan shutdown requests cancel on every running scan and joins its thread for up to 5 s. The ScanDialog **Cancel** button uses the same endpoint. **What a cancel does in the backend:** the endpoint sets the task's cancel token, which the scan pipeline ([core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py)) polls at every stage boundary and inside its hot loops; the task then ends with `"Scan cancelled."` and the output manifest is never written. A cancel during HASH hard-kills the live exiftool processes (`ExiftoolProcess.kill`) before joining their consumer threads ([#561](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/561)). A consumer blocked inside a large batch read only checks the token between queue gets, so without the kill the join timed out and exiftool was left running. The EXIF drain after HASH is cancel-aware the same way (#607), so a cancel there does not wait out exiftool's 60 s read timeout. The bounded read and EXIF queues watch the token too, so a cancel cannot deadlock on a full queue (#564 / #594).
- **Conditions / variants:** **Hard exit (crash / Task Manager kill):** process-mode hash workers are placed in a Windows `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` job object, so they die with the app instead of spinning the disks ([#460](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/460) / #549). The job assignment had been silently inert until [#555](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/555) fixed a handle-lifetime bug: `int(PyHANDLE)` dropped the OS handle before `AssignProcessToJobObject` ran. exiftool is jailed the same way only when the app is **not** already inside another job (`_process_in_any_job`, which fails safe to "in a job"; [#556](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/556) → [#558](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/558)). exiftool is a PAR self-extracting Perl exe whose child interpreter, a grandchild of the app, gets forced into a nested job chain and starved by the outer job's limits. That corrupted the extended EXIF pass under a CI runner / console host: `s42_scoring` NULL `exif_tag_count`. A bare-desktop hard exit reaps exiftool; a job-nested launch skips jailing and relies on the outer job's owner. **Residual (best-effort):** `sendBeacon` is best-effort by spec. If the browser drops it, the scan keeps running and finishes by itself; while it runs, the registry answers 409 to a new scan. A backend cancel-on-SSE-disconnect grace period is intentionally not built: the SSE stream deliberately does not cancel on disconnect, so a reconnecting page can resume.
- **Related:** issue [#468](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/468) (desktop origin), [#687](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/687) (web guard), #703 (cancel on leave). QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s63_late_cancel_and_main_window_guard.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s63_late_cancel_and_main_window_guard.py) — asserts the `beforeunload` guard arms while a scan runs and is dormant when idle and after cancel (a non-vacuity bracket), plus a late HASH-stage cancel that stops cleanly and never writes the manifest; [`qa/web/scenarios/s03_cancel_scan.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s03_cancel_scan.py) (Cancel button). The `pagehide` → `sendBeacon` cancel is unit-covered in `frontend/src/hooks/useBeforeUnloadGuard.test.ts` (beacons only while running with a task id; never with a null task id, on a bfcache stash, or after unmount), since real navigation in Playwright is the `playwright#20624` CI-hang class. Backend: `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py` (HASH cancel kills exiftool, process-pool workers assigned to the kill job, post-HASH cancel exits promptly, bounded-queue cancel does not deadlock), `tests/test_scanner_exif.py` (job-nesting gate).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client; backend cancel and reaping paths code-read)

### Main window — column order/width persistence

- **Entry point:** The resize handle on the right edge of each result-tree column header ([frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx)), persisted by [frontend/src/lib/columnWidths.ts](../frontend/src/lib/columnWidths.ts).
- **Trigger:** User drags a column's resize handle, or double-clicks it.
- **Behaviour:** The width follows the drag live and is written to `localStorage` (`pm.result-tree.column-widths.v1`) once, when the drag ends; a double-click on the handle restores that column's default width. Widths are read back when the store is created, so they survive a page reload and a manifest load. Column **order** is fixed — the column registry's order is the render order, and the web has no drag-to-reorder (the retired desktop client let the user reorder columns). Floor, end-of-drag rules and the fill-column behaviour are in [Web — result-tree column sort and resize persistence](#web--result-tree-column-sort-and-resize-persistence).
- **Conditions / variants:** Storage is per browser profile and origin; Playwright contexts are isolated automatically, so QA needs no reset. In the desktop shell, `launcher.py` starts pywebview without `private_mode=False`, and pywebview's default private mode (WebView2 InPrivate) is documented not to preserve local storage, so there the widths are expected to reset on every launch (code-read, not live-verified; tracked as [#944](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/944)).
- **Related:** [PR #227](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/227) (closes [#214](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/214), desktop origin); web column model [#685](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/685); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s47_column_layout_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s47_column_layout_persist.py); unit tests `frontend/src/lib/columnWidths.test.ts`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Main window — geometry + splitter persistence

- **Entry point:** The drag handle between the result tree and the preview pane (`preview-resize-handle`, [frontend/src/App.tsx](../frontend/src/App.tsx)) and the title bars / corner grips of the full-res viewer, Execute dialog and Set Action dialog. The desktop-shell window itself is created by [launcher.py](../launcher.py).
- **Trigger:** User drags the tree/preview boundary, or moves / resizes one of those overlays.
- **Behaviour:** The splitter's web analog is the preview-pane width. A drag is clamped to [200 px, 60% of the viewport], so a drag cannot squeeze either pane to invisibility (the [#136](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/136) guarantee); a stored width is not re-clamped on load or window resize; the default is 320 px, and the width is stored in localStorage under `panelWidths` — see [Web — preview panel resizable + persistence](#web--preview-panel-resizable--persistence). Each overlay keeps its own position (and, once resized, size) — see [Web — overlay window geometry (movable / resizable / persisted)](#web--overlay-window-geometry-movable--resizable--persisted). The app window has no persisted geometry: the desktop shell always opens a 1280×800 window, and in a browser tab the browser owns the window.
- **Conditions / variants:** Same storage caveat as [Main window — column order/width persistence](#main-window--column-orderwidth-persistence): localStorage is per browser profile and origin, and under the desktop shell's pywebview private mode it is expected not to survive a relaunch (code-read, not live-verified; tracked as [#944](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/944)).
- **Related:** [PR #191](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/191) (closes [#141](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/141), [#136](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/136), desktop origin); web preview resize [#739](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/739). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py) (preview width + viewer geometry) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py) (both dialogs); unit tests `frontend/src/lib/panelWidths.test.ts`, `frontend/src/App.previewResize.test.tsx`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Web — preview panel resizable + persistence

- **Entry point:** Drag handle (`data-testid="preview-resize-handle"`) between the result tree and the preview pane in [`frontend/src/App.tsx`](../frontend/src/App.tsx), wired to `store.setPreviewWidth` in [`frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts`](../frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts) and the persistence helpers in [`frontend/src/lib/panelWidths.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/panelWidths.ts).
- **Trigger:** User drags the thin vertical grabber between the result tree and the preview pane. Restores the persisted width on every page load.
- **Behaviour:** Mirrors the [#685](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/685) column-resize recipe verbatim in structure: `mousedown` on the handle begins tracking `window` `mousemove` (so the drag keeps working once the cursor leaves the thin handle); each move updates the width in-memory only (`persist=false`) for live feedback, and the final width is written to localStorage once at the end of the gesture (`persist=true`) — avoiding a write per pixel of drag. **End of drag ([#851](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/851)):** the drag ends — one persisted write, window listeners removed — on `mouseup`, on window `blur`, on `pointercancel`, or on the first `mousemove` that arrives with no button held; unmounting mid-drag removes them too. The last three cover a button released where the page never hears the `mouseup` (over another application, or a system-cancelled pointer); before that the preview pane kept resizing on every later mouse move over the page, so the layout stuck to the cursor until the user clicked again and the persisted width ended up wherever the cursor happened to be. While dragging, the width is clamped to **[200px, 60% of the current viewport width]** — the 200px floor wins even on a viewport too narrow for the 60% cap — so a drag cannot squeeze either pane to invisibility. A stored width is not re-clamped when the page loads or the window is resized, so a width saved on a wide window can leave the tree very narrow, or hidden, on a smaller one. The default width is **320px** ([#878](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/878) layout slice PV — the pane now carries a title strip, a 4:3 frame, a five-row metadata table and a stacked decision block; 288 is where the zh-TW decision labels start wrapping). A persisted width that is exactly the OLD default (288) migrates to 320 — the drag handler writes the whole map on any resize, so an unresized pane still has a stored 288 that nobody chose; **any other stored width is the user's own and is kept**. The drag handle itself is 4px and transparent at rest, a hairline on hover and the accent while dragging.
- **Conditions / variants:** Persisted under localStorage key **`panelWidths`** — a small per-panel width map (`{"preview": <px>}`), kept as its OWN key separate from the [#685](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/685) column-widths key (`pm.result-tree.column-widths.v1`), since it is a distinct persisted concept. Per-browser, auto-isolated per Playwright context like the column-widths key — no server-side reset needed.
- **Full-res viewer note:** the viewer's own window chrome is no longer a divergence — it became a movable/resizable overlay in the same issue; see "Web — overlay window geometry" below. Its Ctrl+scroll zoom stays clamped **5%–800%** (#743; was 10%–1000%).
- **Related:** issue [#739](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/739), [#743](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/743) (zoom clamp); mirrors the #685 recipe ([`frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx), [`frontend/src/lib/columnWidths.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/columnWidths.ts)). QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py) (re-flip of `s39_window_geometry_persist` from SKIP, `qa/web/scenario_map.yml`); unit tests [`frontend/src/lib/panelWidths.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/panelWidths.test.ts) (persistence) + [`frontend/src/App.previewResize.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/App.previewResize.test.tsx) (drag + end-of-drag, #851). Same end-of-drag rule as the #796 column handle and the #739 overlay geometry — see "Web — result-tree column sort and resize persistence" and "Web — overlay window geometry" below.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-06 (#851 — off-window release ends the drag; the new vitest fails on the pre-fix build with the pane widening 388px → 614px after a `blur` + no-button move, and s39 fails its `blur` control there too); 2026-07-03 (#739 initial implementation); 2026-07-04 (#743 — full-res zoom clamp narrowed to 5%–800%)

### Web — overlay window geometry (movable / resizable / persisted)

- **Entry point:** one shared mechanism — the drag/resize/clamp hook [`frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.ts`](../frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.ts), the persistence helpers [`frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.ts), and the corner grip [`frontend/src/components/ui/overlay-resize-handle.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/ui/overlay-resize-handle.tsx). Applied to three surfaces: the full-resolution viewer ([`FullResViewer.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx), ids `fullres-title-bar` / `fullres-resize-handle`), the Execute Action dialog ([`execute/ExecuteDialog.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/execute/ExecuteDialog.tsx), `execute-title-bar` / `execute-resize-handle`; its title reads **"Execute Actions — Review"** since the 2026-09-14 copy audit (E6) — the dropped "— Review" half is what names the dialog as a gate before destruction) and the Set Action dialog ([`action/ActionDialog.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx), `action-title-bar` / `action-resize-handle`).
- **Trigger:** User drags an overlay's title bar to move it, or its bottom-right corner grip to resize it. The geometry is restored every time that overlay is opened, including after a page reload.
- **Behaviour:** Mousedown on the title bar / grip begins tracking `window` `mousemove`/`mouseup`, so the gesture survives the cursor leaving the grab target; each move updates the geometry in memory only, and localStorage is written **once** per gesture — the same "live updates, one write per gesture" recipe as the #685 column resize and the preview splitter above. The gesture also ends on `blur`, `pointercancel`, or the first move that arrives with no button held, so releasing the button outside the browser window cannot leave an overlay stuck to the cursor (the #796 class). A mousedown that lands on a control inside the title bar (the viewer's ✕ button) clicks that control instead of starting a drag.
  - **Move and resize persist different things.** A **move** stores position only; the dialog keeps its automatic height and goes on growing and shrinking with its content exactly as it does un-moved. Only a **resize** pins a width/height. (Pinning height on a move meant a dragged Set Action dialog could not grow when its Preview block appeared, and the Cancel/Apply row was pushed clean out of the box — measured: dialog bottom 606, Apply bottom 716.) The one exception is the full-res viewer: dragging it while it fills the whole viewport **un-maximizes** it to 80% of the viewport, centered, and then follows the cursor — the OS window-manager convention, and what makes the viewer draggable at all given its fill-the-viewport default. That is safe because the viewer is a flex column whose image area already scrolls.
  - **Scrolling and the size floor.** Both dialogs put everything between the header and the footer in a scrollable body (`execute-body` / `action-body`, `flex: 1 1 auto`, `min-height: 0`). Because the body's flex basis is `auto`, an unpinned dialog is sized by its content exactly as before; once a height *is* pinned, the body shrinks and scrolls while the header and footer stay put, so the primary buttons are always inside the box. The minimum overlay size is **320×280 px**, set from a live measurement: with the Execute dialog's fixed chrome (header + toolbar + all-delete banner + footer ≈ 200 px) a 260 px box left the file tree exactly one row, so the floor sits above that.
  - Escape, the focus trap and each dialog's existing outside-interaction policy are untouched; so are the viewer's Ctrl+scroll zoom clamp (5%–800%, #743) and its drag-to-pan.
  - **Dialogs animate open and closed ([#852](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/852)).** Every Radix dialog (the shared wrapper [`frontend/src/components/ui/dialog.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/ui/dialog.tsx)) fades in and zooms from 95% on open and reverses on close; the full-res viewer's backdrop ([`FullResViewer.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx)) fades the same way. The `animate-in` / `animate-out` / `fade-in-0` / `zoom-in-95` / `data-[state=closed]:…` classes those surfaces already carried emit real CSS only because **`tw-animate-css`** (MIT, exact-pinned 1.4.0 — the Tailwind v4 successor of the `tailwindcss-animate` plugin) is imported in [`frontend/src/index.css`](../frontend/src/index.css). Dialog content runs **200 ms** (its own `duration-200`), backdrops the package default 150 ms. Before #852 no animate plugin was installed, so those class names compiled to nothing and every dialog appeared and vanished instantly. Two consequences worth knowing: a closing dialog stays **mounted until its exit animation ends** (Radix) and its backdrop still swallows pointer events while it fades, so a driver must wait for it to detach rather than assume the DOM is clear on the next tick — `wait_manifest_loaded()` settles on that via `wait_no_modal_overlay()` ([`qa/web/_invariants.py`](../qa/web/_invariants.py)), which is what keeps the raw-mouse drags in s39 / s47 landing on the page instead of on the fading scan-dialog backdrop; and the clamp below now measures the **layout** box (`offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`) instead of `getBoundingClientRect`, which the entry zoom scales for those 200 ms — measured against the scaled box, a restored dialog near the bottom edge opened 22 px low with its footer off-screen (stored y=690 in a 720 px viewport settled at 310, bottom 742).
- **Conditions / variants:** Each surface has its **own** localStorage key — `pm.overlay-geometry.fullres.v1`, `pm.overlay-geometry.execute.v1`, `pm.overlay-geometry.action.v1` — separate from `panelWidths` (preview splitter) and `pm.result-tree.column-widths.v1` (#685), so one corrupt or stale blob cannot disturb the other surfaces. Stored value is `{x, y, w, h}` in CSS pixels, where **`w`/`h` are `null` until the overlay is actually resized**. **Clamp rule:** the saved position is clamped against the element's *rendered* box — on open, on every browser-window resize, and whenever the overlay's own content changes size (a `ResizeObserver`, so a dialog that grows near the bottom of the screen slides back up instead of pushing its footer off-screen). A pinned size is clamped to `[320×280, viewport]` first, then the origin, so the window always sits fully inside the viewport and a geometry saved on a larger monitor can never open with its title bar (the only drag affordance) or its footer buttons out of reach. A blob with an unusable **position** is discarded whole; an unusable **size** degrades to auto, keeping the position the user did choose. Every storage error is swallowed: the surface then renders at its **default** layout — the viewer fills the viewport, the two dialogs stay centered at their existing `max-w-4xl` / `max-w-2xl` size, so a first open is unchanged from before this feature.
- **Related:** issue [#739](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/739) (owner sign-off 2026-07-17: "implement it once, apply to all three surfaces"); closes divergence rows **D6**, **D7** and **D12** in [`docs/audits/web-divergence-list-2026-07.md`](audits/web-divergence-list-2026-07.md). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py) (viewer) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py) (both dialogs, re-flipped from SKIP); unit tests [`frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.test.ts) and [`frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.test.tsx).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-06 (#852 — live-read `getComputedStyle(...).animationName` = `enter`, duration 0.2s on the dialog content and 0.15s on both backdrops, against `none`/`0s` on the pre-change build; restored-near-edge dialog settles at top 288 as it did before the animation); 2026-09-05 (#739 — move persists position only; scrollable dialog bodies + 320×280 floor; live-run via s39/s48)

### Main window — keyboard navigation

- **Entry point:** The main result tree's scroll container (`main-result-tree`, `role="tree"`, `tabIndex=0`, [frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx)) for the arrow-key cursor, and a document-level hook ([frontend/src/hooks/useDecisionShortcuts.ts](../frontend/src/hooks/useDecisionShortcuts.ts), mounted once by [frontend/src/App.tsx](../frontend/src/App.tsx)) for the `d` / `k` decision shortcuts.
- **Trigger:** With the tree focused, press ArrowDown / ArrowUp. Outside a text field and with no dialog or menu open, press bare `d` or `k`.
- **Behaviour:**
  - **Decision shortcuts ([#615](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/615), web [#708](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/708)).** Bare `d` sets the current selection's decision to `delete`; bare `k` clears it to `''` (no decision / keep). Both drive the same `store.setDecisions` the multi-select context menu uses, so a multi-selection is written in one `PATCH /api/decision`, and a locked row in it opens the lock-confirm dialog. Modifier-bearing presses (Ctrl/Cmd, Alt, Shift) fall through. The shortcuts are inert while an editable field (input, textarea, select, contenteditable) has focus or while any modal layer is open (`[role="dialog" | "alertdialog" | "menu"]`, which covers every dialog and the context menus), and with an empty selection they do nothing.
  - **Roving arrow-key cursor ([#709](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/709)).** ArrowDown / ArrowUp move the cursor through the tree in visible row order, group headers included; the first press starts at the top (Down) or the bottom (Up), and the ends clamp. The rows are virtualized, absolutely positioned, non-focusable `<div>`s, so the scroll container keeps DOM focus and names the active row with `aria-activedescendant`, pointing at the row's `role="treeitem"` wrapper; the attribute is dropped while the active row is virtualized out of the DOM, so it never names a missing element. Landing on a **file row** does what a click does (`setSelection` + `setSelectedFile`), so the preview pane and `d` / `k` follow the cursor. Landing on a **group header** selects the group for the grid preview without expanding or collapsing it, and **clears the file selection** — a header is not part of the multi-selection model (see [Web — main result tree multi-selection](#web--main-result-tree-multi-selection)), so `d` / `k` are no-ops there and a decision can never land on a row the user has visibly moved off; clicking a header does the same. Arrowing back onto a file row re-selects it. The cursor is held as a row identity, so it survives a manifest rebuild (a decision write replaces `manifest.groups` wholesale), and it also moves to a clicked row (under any modifier), a right-clicked row, and the first keeper the post-scan auto-select scrolls to. The active row is kept on screen with `virtualizer.scrollToIndex(…, {align: "auto"})`, and the virtualizer is given `scrollPaddingStart` = the measured sticky-header height ([#699](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/699)), so a row scrolled in from above comes to rest at the header's bottom edge instead of underneath it. Modifier-bearing arrows fall through, and the handler is scoped to the tree container, so arrows typed in any input elsewhere never move the cursor.
  - **The cursor is visible (layout slice T, 2026-09-18).** `aria-activedescendant` is heard by a screen reader but not seen, so the cursor row draws a **2 px inset terracotta ring** (`outline: 2px solid #a85a2c; outline-offset: -2px; border-radius: 6px`) plus a **▸ caret** in the row's existing left gutter. Fill = state, stroke = cursor: the ring composes over the selection and hover tints rather than replacing them, so a row can be focused *and* selected. The caret is `aria-hidden` and is what carries the cursor in grayscale and for colour-blind users; it sits inside padding the row already had, so no row moves by a pixel. Both are `:focus-visible`-gated through the scroll container (`group-focus-visible:`) — DOM focus never moves onto a row — so a mouse user never sees the ring, and it never lands on the row's decision control.
- **Conditions / variants:** Group headers are also Tab stops (`role="button"`); Enter or Space toggles one that has DOM focus. Not provided: Shift+arrow range extension, Home / End / PageUp / PageDown and Left / Right expand-collapse do not move the cursor; there is no play/pause key (video uses the player's native controls), and no Alt+F menu mnemonic — Radix and the browser own dialog focus, Esc and Tab order.
- **Related:** QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s26_keyboard_navigation.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s26_keyboard_navigation.py) (branches A–C: `d` / `k` on a single and a multi-selection; branch D: the roving cursor, the group-header stop and the below-header scroll-in) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py) (the ring's computed outline and offset and the caret, after a real key press); unit [`frontend/src/hooks/useDecisionShortcuts.test.ts`](../frontend/src/hooks/useDecisionShortcuts.test.ts) (modifier, editable-field and open-modal guards) and [`frontend/src/components/ResultTree.keyboard.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.keyboard.test.tsx) (cursor order, header stop, rebuild survival, which row carries the ring).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-18 (layout slice T focus ring — live s74: cursor row `outline 2px solid rgb(168, 90, 44)`, offset `-2px`, caret `▸` displayed); 2026-09-06 (#709 web roving arrow cursor — live s26 branch D)

### Main window — results tree double-click

- **Entry point:** File rows and group-header rows of the main result tree — `onDoubleClick` on [frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.tsx) and `onClick` on [frontend/src/components/result/GroupRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/result/GroupRow.tsx), wired in [frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx).
- **Trigger:** The user double-clicks a file row, or clicks a group-header row.
- **Behaviour:** **File row** → opens the in-app full-resolution viewer (`fullres-dialog`, [frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx](../frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx)) on that file via `store.openFullRes`; the double-click's first click has already selected the row. An image opens at native resolution with drag-to-pan and Ctrl+wheel zoom; a video opens in a native-controls player without autoplay — see [Preview pane — full-resolution viewer](#preview-pane--full-resolution-viewer). The retired desktop client opened the file in the OS default viewer instead. **Group-header row** → toggles expand/collapse on a single click (it has no double-click handler), and the same click makes the group the preview target (grid view) while clearing the file selection.
- **Conditions / variants:** Double-clicking the preview pane's image, or an image tile in its grid view, opens the same viewer (see [Preview pane — full-resolution viewer](#preview-pane--full-resolution-viewer) for what a video does). Enter or Space toggles a group header that has keyboard focus. Revealing the file in Explorer is the right-click **Open folder**, not a double-click.
- **Related:** Originally [PR #198](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/198) (closes [#143](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/143)); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s05_huge_preview.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s05_huge_preview.py) (row double-click → the full-res image loads), [`qa/web/scenarios/s68_full_res_viewer_double_click.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s68_full_res_viewer_double_click.py) (preview-tile double-click → the viewer opens; Esc closes it) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s40_results_tree_double_click.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s40_results_tree_double_click.py) (group-header click collapses and re-expands — `aria-expanded` plus member-row visibility); unit [`frontend/src/components/result/GroupRow.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/result/GroupRow.test.tsx) (toggles on a row click and on Enter) and [`frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.test.tsx) (preview double-click calls `openFullRes`).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Main window — row selection (no horizontal auto-scroll)

- **Entry point:** None — removed with the desktop client in #646.
- **Trigger:** None.
- **Behaviour:** The desktop result tree turned off its toolkit's auto-scroll so that selecting a row no longer jerked a horizontally scrolled table sideways. The web tree has no such auto-scroll to turn off: a row click updates only the selection, the preview target and the keyboard cursor, with no scroll call, and the tree's two programmatic scrolls — the arrow-key cursor keeping its row in view ([#709](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/709)) and the one-shot scroll to the first keeper after a scan — go through a vertical-only virtualizer, so neither moves the horizontal position.
- **Conditions / variants:** The web table does scroll horizontally once its columns outgrow the pane (File Name keeps its floor width, then the table scrolls; the sticky column header scrolls sideways with it).
- **Related:** [frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx); keyboard cursor under [Main window — keyboard navigation](#main-window--keyboard-navigation).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Main window — sort persistence within session

- **Entry point:** The **File Name** and **Size** header cells of the result tree ([frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/result/ColumnHeaderRow.tsx)), which call `store.toggleSort`; the chosen column and direction live in the `resultView` slice of [frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts](../frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts).
- **Trigger:** User clicks a sortable column header.
- **Behaviour:** The first click on a column sorts ascending; a repeat click flips the direction. The sort orders rows within each group (group order is unchanged) and is re-applied from the store on every render. It therefore survives every refresh of the loaded rows within the page session — a decision change, an execute run, re-opening a manifest — because loading a manifest replaces the groups but never touches `resultView`. With no sort chosen, rows keep the server's score-descending order within each group (see [Keep-worthiness scoring](#keep-worthiness-scoring)); the client sort is stable, so rows that tie under a column sort keep that score order. Full detail: [Web — result-tree column sort and resize persistence](#web--result-tree-column-sort-and-resize-persistence).
- **Conditions / variants:** Session-only by design: the sort is not stored anywhere, and a page reload or relaunch returns to server order. Only File Name and Size are sortable; Similarity, Action, Score, Resolution and Shot Date are not.
- **Related:** [#121](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/121) (desktop origin); web column model [#685](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/685); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s45_sort_persistence.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s45_sort_persistence.py) (per-group sort, checked against a SQL oracle, surviving an in-session manifest reopen); unit tests `frontend/src/lib/resultColumns.test.ts`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Main window — status bar baseline

- **Entry point:** The footer [frontend/src/components/StatusBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/StatusBar.tsx): the resting message is testid `main-status-bar`, inside the 30 px strip `main-status-strip`; its text (`statusText`) is computed in [frontend/src/App.tsx](../frontend/src/App.tsx) and passed in.
- **Trigger:** Always present; recomputed on every render from the `manifest` and `scan` store slices.
- **Behaviour:** Five mutually exclusive states, first match wins. A loaded manifest shows `"{groups} {groupWord} · {files} {fileWord}"`, with singular or plural picked per count, so a one-group manifest reads "1 group · 1 file" rather than "1 groups · 1 files" (copy audit S1). `manifest.loading` shows "Loading manifest…". A running scan shows "Scanning" plus the localized stage name (#740). A failed scan shows "Scan failed: {error}". Otherwise the bar shows "Ready". Because the text is derived state rather than a timed message, it cannot expire to blank. That rules out the failure class the retired desktop client fixed in [#138](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/138) (the startup message vanished after 3 s) and [#140](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/140) (a menu hover wiped the load summary). With a manifest loaded, the strip also shows #906's "⊗ N marked to delete" count and "↺ reclaims {size}" total, computed over the whole manifest and shown at zero too. A **Density** switch sits at the right end of the strip.
- **Conditions / variants:** Two additive error lines render **below** the strip, so its fixed height cannot clip them. Each is independently gated and carries `role="alert"`. **`main-status-error`** shows `manifest.error` whenever it is non-null; about ten store actions that call the backend write it (load, decision, lock, remove, prune, save, …) (#712). **`main-execute-error`** shows `execute.executeError` whenever it is non-null **and** the Execute dialog is closed — the open dialog shows the same message inline. That line is what makes a failed **Open Folder** reveal from the main tree visible (see [Context menu — Open Folder](#context-menu--open-folder)). Both lines are user-worded (copy audit S2). [frontend/src/lib/apiErrorText.ts](../frontend/src/lib/apiErrorText.ts) maps the closed set of shapes the routes raise onto `web.error.*` sentences. That set covers the 409 `detail.code` values such as `locked_paths` and `execute_already_running`, and the `HTTP <status>: <verb> failed: …` bodies. The raw server text is demoted to a smaller technical line inside the same alert, so both testids still carry the whole message. An unrecognised string is never swallowed: it becomes the detail of a generic sentence.
- **Related:** [#138](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/138), [#140](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/140) (desktop origin), #712, #906; QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s37_status_bar_baseline.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s37_status_bar_baseline.py) (idle on start-up, then the groups · files summary after a scan); unit tests `frontend/src/components/StatusBar.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/apiErrorText.test.ts`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-22 (web-docs-drift backfill — web footer states + `MAIN_STATUS_ERROR`/`MAIN_EXECUTE_ERROR` documented here, consolidated from the one-off mention under Context menu — Open Folder)

### Open Manifest — base flow

- **Entry point:** Menu bar **File > Open Manifest…** (`menu-file-open`) and, while no manifest is loaded, the empty-state **Open Manifest…** button (`main-empty-open`, see [Empty-state action buttons](#empty-state-action-buttons)). Both open the in-page [Web — filesystem browse dialog](#web--filesystem-browse-dialog) (`FsBrowser`, `mode="file"`). A secondary route is the toolbar's manifest-path field (`main-manifest-input`) and its **Open** button (`main-manifest-open-button`) in [frontend/src/components/Toolbar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/Toolbar.tsx). That pair is the first thing the toolbar sheds on a narrow window, since the picker duplicates it.
- **Trigger:** User confirms a file in the picker, or types a path and clicks **Open** or presses Enter.
- **Behaviour:** Every route calls `store.loadManifest(path)` → `GET /api/manifest?path=…` → `load_review`. The picker route also writes the chosen path into the toolbar field; the post-scan auto-load does not, so after a scan the field can still show an older path. The field route trims its input and does nothing on an empty path. The picker is seeded with the field's current value. While a first load runs (no manifest on screen yet), the status bar reads "Loading manifest…"; loading over an already-loaded manifest keeps its summary on the bar until the new one arrives. On success the store replaces the groups and counts wholesale and clears the row selection, and the status bar shows the groups · files summary (see [Main window — status bar baseline](#main-window--status-bar-baseline)). The server also registers the manifest's folders as allowed roots, so the image routes will serve its thumbnails. A failed load (400 empty path, 404 missing file, 422 load error) leaves the previous manifest on screen and shows the error in `main-status-error`. There is no native file-type filter: `FsBrowser` tags `.sqlite` / `.db` files (case-insensitive) with a "manifest" pill but still lists every file.
- **Conditions / variants:** No confirm fires when a manifest is already loaded. Every decision is already on disk (see [Web — manifest decision persistence](#web--manifest-decision-persistence)), so the new manifest simply replaces the old one in the view. The preview pane takes its row from the loaded groups: the single view renders only if the selected path is found there, and the group grid looks its group up by number. So a file from the previous manifest cannot stay rendered — the web form of [#431](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/431). The preview selection itself is not reset, so a group-grid preview whose group number also exists in the new manifest shows that manifest's group. Old manifests without the newer columns auto-migrate on load, and rows missing the cached size / dates / mtime fall back to per-row filesystem reads; re-scan once for the load-time speed benefit.
- **Recycle-bin / system-folder rows are dismissed on open ([#821](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/821)):** every row whose path sits under a walker skip directory (see [Scan walk — recycle-bin / system folders are never walked](#scan-walk--recycle-bin--system-folders-are-never-walked)) is written `outcome='ignored'` when the manifest is opened, using the walker's own rule. The pass runs inside every manifest load, so it also re-runs on each reload after a decision, and is a no-op write once the manifest is clean. The walk-time skip only ever stopped NEW rows; a manifest built before it kept the old ones live — scored, groupable, selectable as a group's keeper, and offered as delete targets `send2trash` then refuses. A rescan does not fix them either: it replaces the manifest file wholesale (losing every decision), so the reconcile is what makes an existing manifest correct without a multi-hour NAS re-scan. **The change is visible in two ways:** the review-set count drops on the first open after upgrading (on the author's 40,774-row manifest, 3,952 rows were dismissed and the loaded record count went 24,531 → 21,498), and a group whose only surviving partner was a recycle-bin file drops below two members and leaves the review set with it (the existing orphan-skip — 584 of those 3,033 rows). It is idempotent (a second open dismisses 0), never re-opens a row already finalised `deleted` — including one finalised by a concurrent execute batch after the reconcile read its candidate list — and touches no filesystem (path strings only, so an offline NAS costs nothing). **It is also fail-soft:** if the manifest cannot be written (a read-only file, a locked DB), the dismissal is skipped with a WARNING naming the manifest and the manifest still opens with those rows live, exactly as before this change — housekeeping the user did not ask for never turns an openable manifest into an unopenable one. Known divergence: the reconcile has no scan root to bound with, so a manifest whose source root sits INSIDE a skip directory has its rows dismissed on open, where the walker would have indexed them; `UPDATE migration_manifest SET outcome='' WHERE …` restores them.
- **Related:** Foundation in [PR #12](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/12); preview clear [#431](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/431) (desktop origin). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s16_open_manifest.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s16_open_manifest.py) (scan → reload → reopen through the `FsBrowser` picker from the empty state; the status bar after reopening equals the one after the scan) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s24_stale_manifest_paths.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s24_stale_manifest_paths.py) (stale paths). Open-time reconcile ([#821](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/821)) pinned by `tests/test_manifest_skip_reconcile.py`; the `outcome` column it writes comes from [#584](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/584). Route `GET /api/manifest` in `app/web/routes/review.py`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-22 (web-docs-drift backfill — toolbar input/Open button + `FsBrowser` file-picker route documented); 2026-09-03 (#821 — skip-directory reconcile on open, measured on the author's 40,774-row manifest)

### Save Manifest Decisions — base flow

- **Entry point:** None — removed with the desktop client in #646. The web menu bar deliberately has no **File > Save Manifest Decisions…** item ([frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx)).
- **Trigger:** None.
- **Behaviour:** The retired desktop client held decisions in memory and wrote them through a file picker, either in place or as a copy at a new path, clearing its dirty flag. The web has no save step and no dirty state: every decision is written to the manifest's SQLite the moment it is made (`PATCH /api/decision`), so there is nothing to save — see [Web — manifest decision persistence](#web--manifest-decision-persistence).
- **Conditions / variants:** The backend keeps `POST /api/save` (`save_manifest` in [core/app_service/execute_service.py](../core/app_service/execute_service.py)). With no `target_path` it runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL)` and returns the number of in-review rows. With a `target_path`, which must pass the allowed-roots check, it checkpoints and then copies the manifest file there. The store wraps it as `saveManifest`, but no component calls it, so it has no UI entry point; a save-as / export-snapshot affordance is the open product question noted in that section.
- **Related:** Desktop dirty-flag plumbing in [PR #158](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/158); web menu-bar parity backlog [#673](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/673) (Save-Manifest sub-item); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s12_save_manifest.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s12_save_manifest.py) (a decision survives page reload + manifest reopen and is committed on disk).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Scan dialog — auto-select after scan

- **Entry point:** **Auto select after scan** checkbox (testid `scan-auto-select`) inside the Scan dialog's **Advanced settings** disclosure — [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx). Sent as `auto_select_enabled` on `POST /api/scan` (`WebScanRequest`, [app/web/models.py](../app/web/models.py)) and applied by the scan pipeline ([core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py)) with the helpers in [core/services/auto_select.py](../core/services/auto_select.py).
- **Trigger:** User expands **Advanced settings**, ticks **Auto select after scan**, and starts the scan. The checkbox defaults **off** and is **not persisted** — it resets to off every time the dialog opens (the autotune checkbox is the only control in that panel that remembers its state).
- **Behaviour:** After scoring, the pipeline picks the top-scored row of each duplicate group (`top_score_path_per_group`) and sets its `action` to `KEEP`, before the manifest summary is printed and the manifest is written. That keeper also gets `user_decision=""` — the canonical keep state ([#425](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/425) fixed an earlier literal-`"keep"` write that leaked into the Action column as raw text) — and `is_locked=1` ([#393](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/393)). Since [#651](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/651) those decision + lock writes run inside `write_manifest`'s own transaction ([scanner/manifest.py](../scanner/manifest.py)), before its single commit and atomic `os.replace`, so a crash mid-write can never leave a manifest on disk whose keepers are unlocked. In the result tree the keeper shows **Keep** in the Action column with its lock toggle on, and its decision buttons stay disabled while it is locked — the same state a manual keep + lock produces. The lock composes with the lock gates: a later bulk **Set Action** (`POST /api/action/bulk-decide`) or **Execute** run that would touch the keeper gets a 409 `locked_paths` and the client asks before overriding (see [Execute Action — lock-confirm dialog](#execute-action--lock-confirm-dialog)), instead of silently clobbering the auto-pick. Other duplicates keep their classifier action (`EXACT` / `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` / …) and the default decision (`user_decision=""`, shown as **Keep**), so deleting anything still takes an explicit user decision. Auto-select picks keepers, never deleters — unless the aggressive sub-option below is also on. The scan log reports `Auto-select: marked N keeper(s) per group.` and, after the write, `Auto-select: locked N keeper(s); decisions written.`
- **Conditions / variants:** Default is off. Ranking is the one shared top-N-by-score rule (`top_n_paths`, [#778](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/778)) that the Set Action "Top N by score" pattern (see [Set Action dialog — numeric comparison panel](#set-action-dialog--numeric-comparison-panel)) and the right-click **Apply best-copy decisions to this group** also use: `score=None` rows are excluded, ties break by `source_path` ascending — so manual and automatic picks converge on the same keeper. The checkbox is disabled while a scan runs. After the scan the dialog selects and scrolls to the keepers (see [Scan flow — visual selection of KEEP rows after scan](#scan-flow--visual-selection-of-keep-rows-after-scan)).
- **Related:** [PR #232](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/232) (closes [#212](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/212)); [#393](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/393) (keep + lock writes and the aggressive sub-option); [#651](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/651) (atomic write); [#778](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/778) (shared ranking). Pinned by `tests/test_auto_select.py` (`TestTopScorePathPerGroup`, `TestApplyAutoSelectDecisions`), `tests/test_scan_runner.py::TestAutoSelectPipeline` (keeper lock, atomic write) and `tests/test_topn_keeper_parity.py`; QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s49_scan_auto_select.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s49_scan_auto_select.py) (keep + lock, keeper highlighted) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py) (aggressive).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Scan dialog — auto-select aggressive ("delete all others")

- **Entry point:** **Also mark all other files for delete** checkbox (testid `scan-aggressive-delete`), indented beneath **Auto select after scan** in the Scan dialog's **Advanced settings** — [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx). Sent as `auto_select_aggressive_delete` on `POST /api/scan`; the eligible rows are chosen by `non_keepers_for_aggressive_delete` in [core/services/auto_select.py](../core/services/auto_select.py).
- **Trigger:** User expands **Advanced settings**, ticks **Auto select after scan**, then ticks the indented sub-option, and starts the scan. Defaults **off**; not persisted (resets to off every time the dialog opens).
- **Behaviour:** Destructive-leaning, opt-in. Alongside the parent's keep + lock on each keeper, every eligible non-keeper in a scored group gets `user_decision='delete'`, written in the same atomic manifest write as the keeper locks ([#651](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/651)). Non-keepers are **not** locked, so they stay editable. When the manifest auto-loads, the triage is already staged — keepers locked on **Keep**, eligible non-keepers on **Delete** — and one **Execute** run carries it out. The scan itself deletes nothing: deletion still goes through the standard Execute flow (`POST /api/execute`, which the client always sends with `recycle: true`, and whose 409 `locked_paths` gate asks before touching any row that was locked after the scan — see [Execute Action — base flow](#execute-action--base-flow) and [Execute Action — lock-confirm dialog](#execute-action--lock-confirm-dialog)).
- **Conditions / variants:** The sub-checkbox is disabled while **Auto select after scan** is off, and the request never carries the aggressive flag without the parent. Unticking the parent also **clears** the sub-option (the retired desktop client kept it for a later re-tick). A non-keeper is eligible only if all of these hold: (a) it is a ranked peer — `group_id` and `score` both set — so `score=None` rows (Live Photo MOV passengers, all-MOV groups) are never tagged; (b) **since [#517](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/517)**, its `match_confidence` is not `"low"` — a near-duplicate matched on pHash alone (no independent dHash agreement) is left for the user to confirm, while byte-identical (SHA) and dHash-confirmed near-dups are `"high"` and stay eligible; (c) **since [#536](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/536)**, the classifier positively flagged it as a duplicate (`action` = `EXACT` or `REVIEW_DUPLICATE`) — a Ref-tier row (`""` / `KEEP` / `UNDATED`) pulled into a group by the unconditional, filename-based pair edge (same-stem RAW+JPG / HEIC+JPG, Live Photo) renders as the `"—"` passenger and is never auto-tagged, so a complementary original can't be silently swept; (d) **since [#824](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/824)**, when a group's keeper is a RAW (`.tif` / `.tiff` included), the best non-RAW row — the one that would have been keeper without the RAW — is protected as well, so a RAW that joins a group through the exact-tier complementary edge can't demote the lossy copy it outscored into the delete set. The same eligibility rule drives the right-click [Web — Context menu: Apply best-copy decisions to this group](#web--context-menu-apply-best-copy-decisions-to-this-group).
- **Related:** [#393](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/393); [#517](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/517) (multi-hash confidence gate); [#536](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/536) (Ref-tier "—" passenger exclusion — only `EXACT`/`REVIEW_DUPLICATE` eligible); [#824](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/824) (RAW keeper protects the best lossy copy); composes with [Scan dialog — auto-select after scan](#scan-dialog--auto-select-after-scan). Pinned by `tests/test_auto_select.py::TestNonKeepersForAggressiveDelete` and `tests/test_scan_runner.py::TestAutoSelectPipeline::test_auto_select_aggressive_delete_marks_non_keepers`; QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py) (sub-option defaults off and can be ticked once the parent is on — the disabled-while-parent-off state is not asserted by any test; keeper locked, every non-keeper duplicate marked delete — keyed off each row's classifier action, since which quality variants classify as duplicates is platform-dependent).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-06-03 (#536 Ref-tier passenger exclusion; #517 confidence gate — backend eligibility rule, unchanged since)

### Scan dialog — stage / throughput / ETA progress (#424)

- **Entry point:** The backend half of scan progress: stage events emitted by the scan pipeline in [core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py) (`_emit_stage` + `_StageTracker`) and streamed to the browser as SSE `stage` events by `SseScanBus.stage` in [app/web/routes/scan.py](../app/web/routes/scan.py). The panel that renders them inside the Scan dialog is documented under [Web — scan progress throughput / ETA / localized stages (#740)](#web--scan-progress-throughput--eta--localized-stages-740).
- **Trigger:** Automatic while a scan runs — at every stage boundary and inside the hot loops.
- **Behaviour:** Each event carries `{stage_name, completed, total, files_per_sec}`. There are six stages, passed as canonical strings — `WALK`, `HASH`, `EXIFTOOL`, `CLASSIFY`, `SCORE`, `WRITE` (the `STAGE_*` constants); the client localises them. Emission is throttled per stage to at most one event per second (`_STAGE_EMIT_INTERVAL_SECONDS`); the first event of a stage, the `completed == total` boundary and forced boundary emits always go out. Throughput is computed backend-side as `(latest_count − oldest_count) / (latest_ts − oldest_ts)` over a 5-second rolling window (`_THROUGHPUT_WINDOW_SECONDS`), and reported as 0 when the window holds fewer than two samples or spans under 0.1 s — so a fast SSD scan doesn't claim a million files/sec on its first event. Samples and events are produced only as work completes, so during a stall no event arrives and the client keeps showing the last rate it received. Per stage: `HASH` reports `completed` out of the total file count; `EXIFTOOL` reports files done against a total that keeps growing while HASH is still routing files to it (JPEGs, whose scoring signals are read in memory, skip exiftool entirely — #786); `WALK` has no upfront total and emits `total=0` with a live running count from the walker's `progress_callback` ([#448](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/448)); `CLASSIFY`, `SCORE` and `WRITE` emit `total=0` when they start and `(1, 1)` when they finish. The client draws an indeterminate bar with an "N files found" count whenever `total` is 0 and a determinate bar otherwise; the ETA is computed client-side and held back until the current stage has run ≥5 s (see the #740 entry). The free-text `log` event stream (the per-line trace — "Hashed N/M", calibration lines, the manifest summary) runs alongside these events and is not affected by them.
- **Conditions / variants:** **Cancel during WALK ([#491](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/491)):** the pipeline hands its cancel token to the walker (`scan_sources(cancel_check=…)`, [scanner/walker.py](../scanner/walker.py)), which polls it at the top of its per-entry loop and between sources, so a **Cancel** lands within about one directory entry instead of waiting for the walk to finish. A post-WALK gate then ends the scan with `failed("Scan cancelled.")` — the same shape as the HASH, post-HASH, CLASSIFY, SCORE, auto-select and WRITE gates — which `SseScanBus.failed` classifies as `cancelled` (any other message is a real `failed`). A scan cancelled before WRITE never writes its output `.db`. **The progress UI never outlives the scan ([#510](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/510)):** the running-state panel is rendered only while `scan.status === "running"`, so every terminal event removes it — on `finished`, `completed_empty` and `cancelled` the dialog closes, and on `failed` the dialog stays open showing the error alert, never a stuck bar. An unreadable entry during WALK is skipped instead of failing the scan ([#509](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/509) — see [Scan walk — skip-on-error traversal](#scan-walk--skip-on-error-traversal)).
- **Related:** [#424](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/424) (stage events + throughput), [#448](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/448) (live WALK count), [#491](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/491) (WALK cancel checkpoint), [#509](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/509), [#510](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/510); client panel [#740](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/740). Pinned by `tests/test_walker.py::TestCancelCheck` + `::TestProgressCallback` (walker hooks), `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestPipelineWalkCancel` + `::TestCancelTokenWiring` (WALK cancel end to end, prior manifest preserved), `tests/test_scan_runner.py::TestRunPipelineHappyPath` (a `WALK` stage event is emitted) and `tests/test_web_scan_bus.py::TestBusFailedClassification` (clean cancel vs error). No unit test currently pins `_StageTracker`'s window / throttle arithmetic. Web scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s03_cancel_scan.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s03_cancel_scan.py) (HASH- and WALK-stage cancel) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s02_empty_folder.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s02_empty_folder.py) (an empty scan closes the dialog with nothing loaded).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)
