### Scan dialog — collapse Advanced Settings

- **Entry point:** **Advanced settings** disclosure (a native `<details>` / `<summary>`, testid `scan-advanced`) in the Scan dialog — [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx).
- **Trigger:** User clicks **Advanced settings** to expand or collapse the panel.
- **Behaviour:** Tuning controls live in one collapsed panel, so the default view is just the source rows, the output path and **Start Scan**. Inside, in order: **Auto-tune reader concurrency (experimental)** (default on — see [read-knee autotune opt-out](#scan-dialog--read-knee-autotune-opt-out)); **Auto select after scan** and its indented **Also mark all other files for delete** (both default off — see [auto-select after scan](#scan-dialog--auto-select-after-scan)); then a **Grouping sensitivity** section with three number inputs — **pHash Similarity Threshold** (default 10, range 2–20, testid `scan-phash-threshold`), **dHash Confidence Threshold** (default 10, range 2–20, [#517](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/517), testid `scan-dhash-threshold`) and **Mean Color Gate** (default 30, range 0–100, testid `scan-color-threshold`). Each threshold shows a short one-line description under its label, with the full technical explanation as a hover tooltip on that description line only — the label itself has no tooltip. The dHash value sets how close the second, independent perceptual hash must be for a pHash near-duplicate to count as high-confidence; it travels as `dhash_threshold` on `POST /api/scan` → `ScanConfig` → `classify(dhash_threshold=…)`. There is no hash-pool control and no hash-workers control: the pool mode is resolved from settings (see [hash pool mode](#scan-dialog--hash-pool-mode)) and worker counts are picked per device (see [hash workers](#scan-dialog--hash-workers-nas-aware-auto-no-ui-control)). Every label, description and tooltip resolves through the `web.scan.*` catalog, and every control is disabled while a scan runs.
- **Near-duplicate threshold floor ([#823](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/823)):** the pHash and dHash inputs run **2–20**, not 1–20. `classify` groups on `0 < distance <= threshold`, and a photographic pHash always carries exactly 32 of its 64 bits, so the Hamming distance between any two of them is **even**: a threshold of 1 admitted only distance-1 pairs — i.e. nothing — so the *strictest* end silently switched near-duplicate detection **off**, and every odd value matched exactly what the even value below it matched (measured: 0 of 86 400 pairwise distances odd over the study's 1 500-hash corpus). A helper line under each hash input (`web.scan.threshold_parity_note`, testids `scan-phash-parity-note` / `scan-dhash-parity-note`) says plainly that an odd value usually matches the same files as the even value below it and that **2 is the strictest useful setting**. The inputs render `min="2"`; because `min` is only a browser hint, `clampThresholdInput` clamps the value when the request is built, and the field snaps to the clamped value on blur and again on **Start Scan**, so what is shown is what runs (details under [Web — ScanDialog source/output persistence](#web--scandialog-sourceoutput-persistence)). The **mean-colour gate is unchanged** (0–100; 0 legitimately means "off"), and **`scanner/dedup.py` is unchanged** — the predicate was right, the control was misdescribing it.
- **Conditions / variants:** The panel opens **collapsed every time** — its expanded state is not persisted. The three thresholds and the two auto-select checkboxes reset to their defaults on every open and are sent per scan only, never written to settings; the autotune checkbox is the one control here whose state persists. The #823 floor started as a UI-only floor; [#876](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/876) moved it to the trust boundary, where a scripted client actually arrives: `WebScanRequest.threshold` and `.dhash_threshold` (`app/web/models.py`) are `Field(ge=…, le=…)`, so a hand-rolled `POST /api/scan` asking for 1 (or 21) gets **422 `Input should be greater than or equal to 2`** instead of a scan that silently finds no near-duplicates, and `/openapi.json` advertises `minimum: 2` / `maximum: 20` on both fields. The bounds are the single pair of constants `NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD_MIN` / `NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD_MAX` in `core/app_service/dtos.py`, which the API model imports; the dialog keeps its own literal 2 / 20 in `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx` (its rendered minimum is asserted by s17, and the API rejects anything outside the shared bounds). The mean-colour gate is not fenced (its 0 legitimately means "off"). A manifest scanned before the floor with `threshold=1` is not rewritten.
- **Related:** [PR #179](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/179) collapsed the grouping parameters into one panel; [#163](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/163) drove the original consolidation; the short-description + hover-tooltip layout first shipped in [#520](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/520); [#736](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/736) added the grouping-sensitivity inputs to the web dialog. Threshold floor [#823](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/823), origin `docs/audits/visual-autoselect-feasibility.md` §3c (popcount-32 parity) + §9 item 6; API bound [#876](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/876). Pinned by `tests/test_web_scan_routes.py::TestNearDupThresholdBounds` (the API bound + the OpenAPI schema), `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.test.tsx` (defaults / min / max, clamp-to-2 in the request, snap on blur and on Start, parity note, thresholds reset on reopen) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py) (rendered input minimums + threshold values reaching the `POST /api/scan` body).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-07 (#823 — pHash/dHash floor raised to 2; live web s17 PASS)

### Scan dialog — folder list (no priority arrows)

- **Entry point:** The **Sources** list in the Scan dialog (testid `scan-source-list`) — rows rendered by [frontend/src/components/scan/SourceRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/scan/SourceRow.tsx), list and ordering in [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx).
- **Trigger:** Always — applies to every interaction with the source list.
- **Behaviour:** Each row is a **Label** input, a **Path** input, a **Browse…** button, a **Recursive** checkbox and a **✕** (*Remove source*) button; **+ Add source** appends a blank row. The rows are **displayed** sorted alphabetically by path, case-insensitively. The underlying list stays in insertion order, and that is the order the scan request carries — so the scanner's source-priority inference (first-listed source = highest priority, `scanner/dedup.py`) follows the order the sources were added, not the order they are shown. Each row's testids (`scan-source-{idx}-label` / `-path` / `-recursive` / `-browse`) use its insertion index, so they stay stable when the display re-sorts.
- **Conditions / variants:** There are no ↑/↓ priority arrows (removed in [#213](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/213)) and no duplicate-path check. Every control on a row is disabled while a scan runs.
- **Related:** [PR #223](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/223) (closes [#213](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/213)); [#736](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/736) (display-only alphabetized list in the web dialog); `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.test.tsx` ("renders source rows alphabetized by path (display-only) while the start-scan payload keeps insertion order"); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py) (alphabetized-display phase) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s10_multi_source.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s10_multi_source.py) (the first-configured source wins cross-source ties).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-03 (#736 — web alphabetized source list; s17 extended)

### Scan dialog — exiftool workers (setting-only)

- **Entry point:** `scan.exif_workers` key in `settings.json` — read by `WebScanRequest.resolved_with()` ([app/web/models.py](../app/web/models.py)) at the start of every `POST /api/scan` that does not carry its own `exif_workers` (the Scan dialog never sends one), then clamped by `ScanConfig` ([core/app_service/dtos.py](../core/app_service/dtos.py)).
- **Trigger:** Always — resolved on every scan start.
- **Behaviour:** Sets how many parallel `ExiftoolProcess` instances the EXIF stage runs ([core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py)): one consumer thread per instance, all draining the single producer-consumer queue the HASH stage feeds ([#450](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/450)), each sending exiftool 500-file batches. Default 2 — also the fallback when the setting is missing or not an integer; clamped to `[1, min(4, cpu_count() // 2)]` in `ScanConfig.__post_init__`. The scan log names the count (`… N parallel process(es)…`). exiftool is single-threaded within one `-stay_open` instance, so parallel instances are what scale the stage (near-linearly up to ~4 on a modern CPU, per [#451](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/451)).
- **Conditions / variants:** No UI control — raise the value in `settings.json` (the key is also on the `/api/settings` allowlist, so `PATCH /api/settings` can set it). A request that sends `exif_workers` explicitly is used as-is (still clamped). This is intentionally a *safe rollout knob* per the issue body, not a power-user control. The EXIF queue is bounded at `2 × 500 × exif_workers` entries ([#564](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/564)) so a slow exiftool can't grow it without limit. On cancel the pipeline kills the live exiftool processes ([#561](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/561)), drains the queue, posts one sentinel per consumer and joins each with a 5 s timeout — no orphaned exiftool processes.
- **Related:** [#451](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/451); [#450](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/450); [#564](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/564); [#561](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/561); [#652](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/652) (web read-back of the setting). Pinned by `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestPipelineExifWorkers` (clamp + one consumer thread per worker), `::TestBoundedExifQueueCancelSafety`, `::TestPostHashCancelKillsExif`, and `tests/test_web_scan_routes.py::TestExifWorkersResolution` (int coercion, fallback to 2).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-03 (#652 — web read-back)

### Scan dialog — read-knee autotune opt-out

- **Entry point:** **Auto-tune reader concurrency (experimental)** checkbox (testid `scan-autotune`) in the Scan dialog's **Advanced settings** — [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx); the same preference is also a checkbox in the Settings dialog ([frontend/src/components/SettingsDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/SettingsDialog.tsx)). Sent as `autotune_read_knee` on `POST /api/scan`; the ramp runs in [core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py), with the measurement logic in [scanner/autotune.py](../scanner/autotune.py).
- **Trigger:** Autotune runs by **default** on every scan — no action needed. To use the static reader count instead, expand **Advanced settings** and untick the checkbox. The choice persists as `ui.scan_dialog.autotune_read_knee` ([#743](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/743)): read when the dialog opens (on when unset) and written back on **Start Scan**.
- **Behaviour:** The scan measures each physical device's read-concurrency knee instead of using the static `NAS=8 / spinning-HDD=1 / else min(4, cpu)` guess: it widens that device's active-reader budget along `1→2→4→8` (never beyond the static count), measures files/s at each level, and settles on the concurrency where doubling stops paying off (less than 15 % gain). A measured knee is cached per `device_key` in `scan.read_knee_cache` (stamped with the autotune recipe version) and reused on later scans of that device: `WebScanRequest.resolved_with()` ([app/web/models.py](../app/web/models.py)) reads the whole cache into the request whenever the request doesn't supply knees, and a device with a cached knee starts straight at it, with no ramp. Spinning HDDs stay pinned at a single reader. Reader concurrency never changes which duplicates are found — only read speed — because `idx` is threaded through the read→compute pipeline, so completion order can't reach `group_id` ([#526](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/526)/[#538](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/538) lex-min determinism).
- **Conditions / variants:** Default **on** (opt-out, experimental). A device ramps only when it has at least `_RAMP_MIN_SCAN_FILES` eligible image files (1584 — videos, GIFs and skipped files don't count; the conservative N=8 floor that bounds the worst-case first-scan sub-MAX read tax to 12.5 %) and a static count above 1; otherwise it runs at the static count with no ramp. The ramp only ever widens, and never past the static count, so it can under-use a device while climbing but never over-subscribe it — and it never changes results. A knee is written to the cache only when that device's ramp settled on a usable measurement **and** it was the only device ramping in that scan (a contention-free measurement); otherwise the next scan ramps again. A malformed or stale-recipe cache entry counts as a miss. The case it helps is hardware the static guess mis-fits (a low-channel NAS that 8 over-subscribes, an SSD under-served by `min(4, cpu)`); on hardware the static guess already fits, the knee equals the static value and it is a no-op. At the API boundary `autotune_read_knee` defaults to **false**, so a hand-rolled `POST /api/scan` that omits it scans without the ramp.
- **Related:** [#551](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/551) (Phase 4 — flipped the default to on / opt-out; Phase 3 added the dialog control; Phases 1–2 landed the knee logic and the in-pipeline ramp); [#652](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/652) (web read-back of `scan.read_knee_cache`); [#743](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/743) (the web checkbox persists its state). Pinned by `tests/test_autotune.py` (knee math), GATE-1 `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestPipelineReadKneeRamp` (the real ramp finds the knee on a synthetic cliff), GATE-2 `tests/integration/test_autotune_ab.py` (the no-regression A/B), `tests/test_web_scan_routes.py::TestCalibrationReadback` + `::TestBusReadKneeMeasured` (web read-back and persist) and `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.test.tsx` ("loads the persisted auto-tune-read-knee preference on open (#743)"); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s66_autotune_read_knee.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s66_autotune_read_knee.py) (autotune at default-on still yields the correct grouping).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-03 (#652 — web read-back)

### Scan dialog — hash pool mode

- **Entry point:** `scan.hash_pool` key in `settings.json` (power-user escape hatch, default `"auto"`) — resolved by `WebScanRequest.resolved_with()` ([app/web/models.py](../app/web/models.py)) on every `POST /api/scan` that leaves `hash_pool` unset (the Scan dialog never sets it), and acted on by the HASH stage in [core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py). There is no user-facing control: calibration is always on ([#560](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/560)).
- **Trigger:** Always — resolved on every scan start.
- **Behaviour:** Selects the executor for the HASH stage. `"thread"` runs the per-file hash work on in-process thread pools. `"process"` runs the picklable `run_hash_for_record` on `ProcessPoolExecutor`s so CPU-bound hashing escapes the GIL across cores. `"auto"` (the default) times a sample (up to 96 files, stratified across devices) of the *real* scan data through both executors, **projects each to the actual file count** (`thread_per_file × N` vs `spawn_cost + process_per_file × N`) and runs the lower one — so process's one-time spawn cost is charged once against the whole run, not against the sample. Process's per-file rate and spawn cost are measured separately with a cold/warm two-batch split; the projected totals and components go to the scan log. With `"auto"`, the `Hashing … (workers=…, pool=…)` log line names the resolved `thread` or `process`; any other setting is logged as written (an unknown value such as `garbage` shows as `pool=garbage`). Any value other than `"auto"` or `"process"` runs on threads. Outcome routing (corrupt-file skips, EXIF-queue handoff) is identical in both modes — in process mode the parent drains completed futures and routes them, since a worker subprocess can't touch the cancel token or the queue. The same calibration pass also times the grouping stage's micro-rates and derives this machine's BK-tree floor from them ([#526](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/526)).
- **Fingerprint cache (PR3b):** the first `"auto"` scan's measured rates are stored in `settings.json` under `scan.hash_pool_cache`, keyed by `hash_pool_fingerprint` — a digest of `cpu_count`, the sorted source paths + recursive flags, and the `HASH_RECIPE_VERSION` / `GROUPING_STRATEGY_VERSION` tokens. `SseScanBus.hash_pool_measured` ([app/web/routes/scan.py](../app/web/routes/scan.py)) writes the entry the moment calibration finishes — before the long hash pass, so it survives a mid-scan cancel — and `resolved_with()` looks it up under the identical fingerprint on the next scan. A later scan of the same library on the same machine reuses the cached rates, re-projecting them to the *current* file count without the ~2 s measurement, so the pick still adapts if the file count changed. A different machine or folder set, a recipe-token bump, or a malformed cache entry misses and re-measures.
- **Always-on calibration ([#560](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/560)):** `"auto"` is the default and there is **no user-facing toggle** — every scan either **reuses the cached rates** (cache hit) or **calibrates silently** (cache miss) before the hash pass, since the per-scan cost is low. `scan.hash_pool` = `"thread"` / `"process"` in `settings.json` still overrides `"auto"` verbatim (no fingerprint, no calibration).
- **Conditions / variants:** Because `"auto"` decides per scan, the Windows `spawn` cost (re-importing PIL/rawpy in each worker, ~150–300 ms each) is only paid when process actually wins. Calibration is skipped (→ `thread`) when fewer than 24 files are queued and there is no cached entry, since spawn/pickle overhead swamps the signal on tiny scans. In process mode each physical device gets its own process pool, sized like the thread-mode readers ([#610](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/610)). Cancellation in process mode stops submitting work and cancels queued files (`shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)`). [#549](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/549)(b): the process branch uses an explicit `try/finally` with `shutdown(wait=False)` rather than `with ProcessPoolExecutor()` — whose `__exit__` runs `shutdown(wait=True)` and would hold a cancelled scan until every in-flight `read_bytes()` finished — so a cancel returns promptly; #549(a) assigns each pool's worker processes to the kill-on-close job so an ungraceful exit of the app reaps them too.
- **Multi-device + NAS shortcut ([#554](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/554) / [#609](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/609) — direction inverted 2026-06-08):** when `"auto"` is active and the scan spans **two or more physical devices with at least one remote (NAS)**, the thread-vs-process calibration is **skipped** and `"process"` is chosen unconditionally (a cached grouping floor is still applied). #554 originally returned `"thread"` here on the bet that per-device thread I/O overlap (HDD 1 reader + NAS 8 readers) would dominate. The 2026-06-08 remediation measured the bet on the real D+J workload with per-second pipeline instrumentation and found the opposite: the shared compute pool is GIL-bound, effective parallelism plateaus at ~2-4 slots, and mixed big+small file interleave (NAS small JPEGs queueing behind 100-130 MB ProRAW DNGs in the shared read→compute queue) triggers 70-second stretches with zero compute completions. The per-device read overlap is real but downstream-gated by the GIL-bound compute pool, so it doesn't pay back. D+J 3400-file apples-to-apples: thread → 601 s timeout (89 % done, projects to ~678 s full) vs process → 421 s complete = **1.6× faster end-to-end**. Single-device and all-local scans are unaffected — calibration still runs and thread can legitimately win (no GIL contention, spawn cost > GIL escape gain). Decision record: `docs/audits/scanner-perf-mixed-workload-process-pool-2026-06-08.md`.
- **Related:** [#486](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/486) (PR1 extracted the picklable compute path); [#652](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/652) (web read-back of `scan.hash_pool` and the cache). Pipeline contract pinned by `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestHashPoolSetting` (incl. unknown value → thread) + `::TestHashPoolCalibration` (projection, small-scan skip, fingerprint + recipe-version invalidation, `test_store_hash_pool_rates_round_trips_through_settings`, multi-device+NAS → process) + `::TestScanTeardownGaps` (no `shutdown(wait=True)` on cancel, kill-job assignment, per-device process pools); web persist + read-back by `tests/test_web_scan_bus.py::TestBusHashPoolMeasured` and `tests/test_web_scan_routes.py::TestCalibrationReadback` (incl. a fingerprint-symmetry pin). Real cross-process spawn is validated by real-world runs, not CI.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-03 (#652 — web read-back)

### Scan dialog — hash workers (NAS-aware auto, no UI control)

- **Entry point:** None in the UI — the Scan dialog has no worker-count control. Per-device counts come from [`scanner/workers.py`](../scanner/workers.py) `hash_workers_for_root` and are applied by the HASH stage in [core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py); device identity comes from `device_key` in [infrastructure/device_key.py](../infrastructure/device_key.py) (re-exported by `scanner/workers.py`).
- **Trigger:** Computed fresh from the scanned files' devices at the start of every HASH stage; no manual override.
- **Behaviour (#548 — per-device concurrent pools):** Records are partitioned by physical device (`device_key`, e.g. `D:` vs `J:`), and the HASH stage runs one reader pool **per device, all concurrently**, each sized by `hash_workers_for_root`: NAS (Windows network drive) → 8, local **spinning HDD** → 1 (single sequential reader, seek-minimising — the disk is the bottleneck at ~97 % active / 38 % CPU, and one reader keeps the head moving sequentially without inter-file seek bouncing; two readers still bounce the head between two concurrently-open files), other local (SSD / NVMe / unknown) → `min(4, os.cpu_count())`. On a mixed scan (local HDD + NAS) the NAS-latency-bound reads overlap the HDD-seek-bound reads instead of queueing behind them, and the HDD itself is no longer over-subscribed. The scan log lists the per-device count and file count (e.g. `D:=1×<n>f, J:=8×<m>f`). The HASH **pool type** (thread vs process) is a separate axis chosen by the [#486 calibration](#scan-dialog--hash-pool-mode), whose sample is stratified across devices so the pick is fair on a mixed scan. **Process** mode also runs one pool per device with the same counts ([#610](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/610)). The request's flat `workers` value (default 4 — the dialog never sends it) now only sizes the calibration sample's executors and appears in the `Hashing … (workers=…)` log line. **NAS server collapsing ([#565](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/565)):** `device_key` maps all Windows drive letters that resolve to the same physical NAS server into **one `\\SERVER` bucket** — e.g. H: and J: on `\\Nas-Host` both become `\\NAS-HOST` and share a single 8-reader pool. Before #565 each mapped letter produced its own device bucket (H: = 8 readers, J: = 8 readers = 16 concurrent SMB reads hammering one NAS box — over-subscription). The collapsing uses `WNetGetConnectionW` via an injectable resolver, memoized per letter per process. Native UNC source paths on the same server (e.g. `\\SRV\share1` and `\\SRV\share2`) also collapse to `\\SRV` without a resolver call. `is_remote_drive` returns True for bare `\\SERVER` keys so `hash_workers_for_root` still yields 8 for the collapsed bucket. Fail-open: any resolver error, non-Windows, or unresolvable letter → falls back to the per-letter key (H: and J: stay distinct), never crashes.
- **Read/compute pipeline split ([#566](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/566)):** in **thread** mode the HASH stage no longer fuses I/O and CPU in one per-file task. Each file flows through a two-stage pipeline: per-device **reader** pools (the #548 counts — HDD=1, NAS=8) each issue one `read_bytes()` (`read_for_record`) whose result goes into a bounded read→compute queue (`maxsize=128`), and a single `cpu_count`-wide **compute** pool drains it (`compute_from_bytes` — SHA + decode + pHash/dHash, the same recipe `run_hash_for_record` uses). This decouples disk-read latency from CPU decode: a reader blocked on an HDD seek no longer stalls a core that could be decoding, and the NAS readers keep the buffer full while the CPU saturates. On a real mixed HDD+NAS scan this measured **~2.3× faster with the throughput sawtooth (忽高忽低) roughly halved** — the fused path serialised read+decode within each task, so utilisation oscillated. Invariants preserved: the single-read guarantee (#446) holds (`read_for_record` reads once; `compute_from_bytes` works from those bytes, RAW via `rawpy.imread(io.BytesIO(data))` — #591 replaced the dead `rawpy.open_buffer` call; video/gif/skip carry `data=None` and stream SHA from disk so the #453 RAM ceiling holds); `idx` is threaded through both stages so results scatter to `hash_results[idx]` in walk order, keeping `classify()` determinism (lex-min `group_id`, the rescore key) unchanged. Cancellation composes with #561 — a cooperative `put(timeout=)` + cancel-token recheck plus `_drain_queue_nowait` release any reader wedged on a full queue before the compute sentinel and the exif kill/sentinels run. **Process** mode + calibration keep the fused `run_hash_for_record`; the split lives only in the thread path. **RAM backpressure ([#587](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/587) / [#596](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/596), replacing #570's task-count semaphore):** `ThreadPoolExecutor.submit()` never blocks, so a queue bound alone cannot cap memory, and a count cap still allows 128 × 130 MB DNGs in flight. Each reader therefore reserves its file's bytes against a byte budget before the bytes enter the queue, and the compute done-callback releases them. The budget is half of physical RAM clamped to 256 MiB–2 GiB (1 GiB if RAM can't be probed), split equally per device so one slow large-file device can't starve another's readers; a single file larger than its slice is admitted alone rather than deadlocking (`scanner/byte_budget.py`). **#569 — JPEG decode shrink-on-load:** `compute_from_bytes` calls `Image.draft("RGB",(256,256))` before `convert()`, decoding JPEG/MPO at ~1/4 resolution (a no-op on PNG/WebP/HEIC/RAW) — ~4× faster JPEG decode. pHash/dHash/mean-color all downsample far below 256px so grouping is unchanged (A/B on 597 real JPEGs: 0 over the threshold, 0 group-membership flips; true `px_w`/`px_h` are read before `draft()` mutates the size). The win lands on **warm re-scans (~5–6×) and compute-bound hardware**; a read-bound first scan is unchanged (its bottleneck is disk/NAS read, not decode). **#822 — EXIF Orientation is applied before pHash/dHash:** `ImageOps.exif_transpose` runs between `draft()` and `convert()` on every non-RAW path, and on the RAW embedded preview (which carries the camera's Orientation in its own EXIF — measured: `raw.sizes.flip` equals that tag on every sampled DNG, and the `postprocess` full-decode fallback needs no transpose because LibRaw applies the flip itself). Previously no orientation was applied anywhere in the hasher, so a copy whose rotation had been baked into its pixels (an export, an edit, a messenger re-save) hashed differently from its EXIF-rotated original and the pair **missed the near-duplicate tier entirely** — a silent false negative on the 19.4 % of this library that carries a non-identity Orientation tag. A/B on 200 real NAS files: all 50 files with a *valid* non-identity Orientation (6/3/8) now hash as their oriented pixels; the 20 carrying the out-of-spec value `0` are correctly left unrotated (Pillow maps only 2–8 to a transpose, matching Explorer/Lightroom). **#826 — the RAW path opens LibRaw once and hashes a reduced preview:** the embedded preview and `raw.sizes` now come off the same handle (it used to open LibRaw twice per file — 0.34–0.38 s per file on 124–129 MB iPhone ProRAW DNGs with the bytes already in RAM), and that preview goes through the same `draft("RGB",(256,256))` the JPEG path uses. On ProRAW the preview is the **full-sensor 8064×6048 = 48.8 MP** frame, not the "1024×768 thumbnail" the old code comment assumed, so pHash was running on ~64× the needed pixels; measured on 25 real DNGs the per-file compute (bytes already in RAM) drops **429 ms → 133 ms median**. True `px_w`/`px_h` still come from `raw.sizes`, never from the preview. **Both changes move hash values, so they share ONE `HASH_RECIPE_VERSION` bump (`"3"` → `"4"`)**; an Orientation-1 non-RAW file is bit-identical to recipe `"3"` (A/B: 105/105 exact parity, pinned in CI by `tests/test_hasher.py::TestHashRecipeParity`). **What the bump does:** the token has exactly one code consumer — `hash_pool_fingerprint` (`core/app_service/scan_runner.py`) — so it invalidates the #486 calibration cache and nothing else. No stored manifest row is keyed to the recipe, so **an existing manifest gets no staleness signal**: its rows keep their recipe-`"3"` hashes and its rotated pairs stay ungrouped **until the user re-scans that library**. Folding both changes into one bump is what makes that re-scan necessary once rather than twice.
- **Conditions / variants:** NAS detection uses Windows `GetDriveTypeW`; the local spinning-disk cap uses the `IncursSeekPenalty` storage IOCTL (`disk_incurs_seek_penalty`, behind an injectable seam). Both fail open: off Windows, or on any detection failure / non-drive-letter root, the device falls through to the SSD-safe `min(4, cpu)` — so a single-device SSD-only scan runs one pool at the historical local count, and a misdetected device is never throttled below that default. Only a **confirmed** spinning HDD gets the single-reader cap. There has been no worker-count control since the [#449](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/449) spinbox was removed, and nothing reads a legacy `scan.workers` setting (orphaned, harmless). Runtime per-device concurrency *calibration* (measuring each device's knee instead of classifying it) shipped separately as [#551](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/551) — see [Scan dialog — read-knee autotune opt-out](#scan-dialog--read-knee-autotune-opt-out).
- **Related:** [#449](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/449) (introduced the spinbox, since removed); [#548](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/548) (per-device concurrent pools); [#452](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/452) (the WALK stage's sibling per-source parallelism); [#565](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/565) (NAS server collapsing); [#566](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/566) (read/compute pipeline split — ~2.3× on mixed HDD+NAS); [#587](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/587) / [#596](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/596) (per-device byte budget); [#610](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/610) (per-device process pools). Helper tests in [`tests/test_scanner_workers.py`](../tests/test_scanner_workers.py), [`tests/test_device_key.py`](../tests/test_device_key.py) and [`tests/test_byte_budget.py`](../tests/test_byte_budget.py); per-device partitioning + ordering + cancellation, the read/compute split's deadlock-safety and idx-order determinism, and the byte-budget bound in [`tests/test_scan_pipeline.py`](../tests/test_scan_pipeline.py) (`TestPipelinePerDeviceHashPools`, `TestStratifiedSample`, `TestBoundedPipelineDeadlockSafety`, `TestPipelineIndexOrderDeterminism`, `TestByteBudgetPipelineBound`, `TestScanTeardownGaps::test_process_branch_uses_per_device_pools`); hash-recipe parity in `tests/test_hasher.py::TestHashRecipeParity`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-03 (#822 EXIF Orientation before pHash/dHash + #826 RAW single LibRaw open and reduced preview — one shared `HASH_RECIPE_VERSION` bump `"3"` → `"4"`; A/B on 200 real NAS files: 105/105 Orientation-1 parity, 50/50 valid-orientation files now hash oriented, RAW compute 429 ms → 133 ms median)

### Scan dialog — multi-source scan

- **Entry point:** The **Sources** rows and the **+ Add source** button (testid `scan-add-source-button`) in the Scan dialog — [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx), rows in [frontend/src/components/scan/SourceRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/scan/SourceRow.tsx).
- **Trigger:** User clicks **+ Add source** to append a blank row, then types a **Label** and a **Path** — or clicks that row's **Browse…** to pick a folder in the in-app filesystem picker (`FsBrowser` in directory mode, opened over the still-open Scan dialog; see [Web — filesystem browse dialog](#web--filesystem-browse-dialog)). Repeats for as many sources as needed, and ticks **Recursive** per source.
- **Behaviour:** One scan covers every source: the request maps each row's label to its path (`sources`) and to its Recursive flag (`recursive_map`). With more than one source the pipeline walks them in parallel, one thread per source ([#452](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/452)), then concatenates the results in source order, hashes every file, and writes one consolidated manifest at the **Output path**. A recursive source walks all subdirectories; an unticked one scans only the folder's immediate files. New rows start **non-recursive**.
- **Conditions / variants:** **Start Scan** enables only when the Output path is filled and at least one row has both a Label and a Path; rows missing either are left out of the scan. Picking a folder with **Browse…** fills only the Path, so a new row still needs a Label typed in (rows re-loaded from settings take the folder name as their label). The request is keyed by label, so two rows with the same label collapse into one source — the later row's path wins. The source list (paths + Recursive flags) and the output path persist to `settings.json` (`sources.list` / `sources.output`) on **Start Scan** — see [Web — ScanDialog source/output persistence](#web--scandialog-sourceoutput-persistence). A missing source path is not checked up front: the scan fails and the dialog shows an alert naming the path — see [Web — scan dialog invalid source path](#web--scan-dialog-invalid-source-path).
- **Related:** [PR #17](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/17) (dynamic multi-source scan); [#452](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/452) (parallel per-source walk); `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestPipelineParallelWalk` (records keep source order even when the second source's walk finishes first); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s10_multi_source.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s10_multi_source.py) (two sources: cross-source dedup, the first-configured source wins ties) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py) (row Browse opens the picker over the dialog).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Scan flow — manifest summary in progress log

- **Entry point:** `print_summary()` in [scanner/manifest.py](../scanner/manifest.py) — called by the scan pipeline ([core/app_service/scan_runner.py](../core/app_service/scan_runner.py)) with its stdout captured; each line goes out as an SSE `log` event and shows in the Scan dialog's running-state log pane (testid `scan-progress-log`, [frontend/src/components/scan/ScanProgress.tsx](../frontend/src/components/scan/ScanProgress.tsx)).
- **Trigger:** A scan that found files finishes scoring (and auto-select, when on); the summary is emitted just before the manifest WRITE stage.
- **Behaviour:** The log pane gets a "Migration Manifest Summary" table with one row per action bucket (kept, exact duplicates, near-duplicates (review), no shot date), each showing the count and percentage. Since #433 dropped the `MOVE` action, unique non-duplicate files carry the empty action (`""`) and roll up into the trailing `other` line rather than a dedicated bucket. The headline `Indexed in manifest` line counts manifest rows; `Skipped (unreadable)` reconciles it against the "Hashed N/M" and "Skipped N unreadable file(s)" lines earlier in the log. A second "Group Summary" block follows with group count, files-in-groups and isolated-file counts. With auto-select on, its keepers are counted as "kept", because the pipeline promotes them to `KEEP` before the summary is printed.
- **Conditions / variants:** **The summary is transient in the web client.** The log pane exists only while the scan is running, and on `finished` the dialog closes itself and clears the scan state — so the summary is on screen only while the WRITE stage runs, usually a moment. The pipeline's log lines go only to the SSE stream (the server keeps the last 500 events for reconnects until the finished task is reaped) — not to the application log. An empty scan never gets here: it ends with `completed_empty` first. Bucket labels come from the `manifest_summary:` keys in [`translations/en.yml`](../translations/en.yml) / [`translations/zh_TW.yml`](../translations/zh_TW.yml), so raw action strings (`KEEP` / `EXACT` / `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` / `UNDATED`) never appear — but the web server never initialises a backend locale, so `t()` falls back to English and a zh_TW user still sees English bucket labels; the `Indexed in manifest`, `Skipped (unreadable)` and Group Summary lines are hardcoded English. The legacy `MOVE` bucket (and its `manifest_summary.move` label) went with the photo-transfer `dest_path` contract in #433. The `Skipped (unreadable)` row is omitted when the count is zero.
- **Related:** [PR #310](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/310) (fix for [#242](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/242)); [#87](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/87) (headline label + skipped reconciliation); [#433](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/433) (MOVE bucket + `dest_path` column removal). Output pinned by `tests/test_scanner_manifest.py::TestPrintSummary`. The web scenarios deliberately don't probe the scan log for content like this, because the panel unmounts on `finished` (see the notes in [`qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s57_scan_auto_select_aggressive.py) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s66_autotune_read_knee.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s66_autotune_read_knee.py)).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Scan walk — skip-on-error traversal

- **Entry point:** `_iter_tree` in [scanner/walker.py](../scanner/walker.py) — the directory traversal primitive behind the WALK stage.
- **Trigger:** Fires automatically during every scan when the walker descends into a source tree that contains an inaccessible entry — typically a broken symlink / junction reparse point (e.g. `node_modules\.bin\acorn`).
- **Behaviour:** The walker used to be `root.rglob("*")` / `root.glob("*")`. `rglob` is a generator: when recursive descent reached an unreadable reparse point, `os.scandir` raised `OSError` [WinError 1920] *from inside* the generator, the exception escaped the walk, and the **entire scan failed on the first bad entry** — even though thousands of good files remained, and the per-path guards (symlink / skip-dir / extension filters) never got a chance to run. The traversal is now a manual `os.scandir` stack: each `scandir` call and each `entry.is_dir()` probe is wrapped in `try/except OSError`, so one inaccessible entry is logged once (a `WARNING` naming the path, in the application log — not in the Scan dialog's log pane) and skipped while the walk keeps going. All the per-path guards (the #491 cancel checkpoint, the #169 Win32-unsafe-name warning, the symlink / skip-directory / `SKIP_FILENAMES` / `MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` filters, the #448 progress callback, and the `limit` cutoff) run unchanged on the new traversal.
- **Conditions / variants:** A symlinked *directory* is not descended into at all (`entry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False)`), so files buried under it are skipped one step earlier than the per-file `_traverses_symlink` guard would — same end result. A Windows **junction** is different: Python does not report it as a symlink (`is_symlink()` is False), so it passes `is_dir(follow_symlinks=False)`, the walker descends into it, and the per-file guard does not exclude its files (CPython semantics, not live-probed; the walker's own docstring says junctions are excluded). An unreadable *root* (or a subdirectory that becomes unreadable mid-descent) is logged once and yields its partial result rather than failing the scan; if nothing readable is left, the scan ends as an empty scan. A source root that does not exist at all is different: `scan_sources` raises `FileNotFoundError` and the scan fails (see [Web — scan dialog invalid source path](#web--scan-dialog-invalid-source-path)).
- **Related:** [#509](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/509); the stuck-progress-bar symptom this looked like is [#510](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/510) (see [Scan dialog — stage / throughput / ETA progress](#scan-dialog--stage--throughput--eta-progress-424)). Walker contract pinned by `tests/test_walker.py::TestScanAbortResilience`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-06-01 (#509 — walker traversal, unchanged since)

---

### Scan walk — recycle-bin / system folders are never walked

- **Entry point:** `has_skip_directory_ancestor` + `SKIP_DIRECTORIES` in [scanner/walker.py](../scanner/walker.py) / [scanner/media.py](../scanner/media.py) — the per-path guard in the WALK stage.
- **Trigger:** Fires automatically during every scan, for every candidate media file whose path has an ancestor directory named (case-insensitively) `$RECYCLE.BIN`, `System Volume Information`, `.Trashes`, `.Trash`, or `#recycle`.
- **Behaviour:** The file is not indexed — it never reaches hashing, grouping, scoring or the manifest. Everything under those folders is already-deleted or OS-managed scratch space; including it also breaks the delete flow, because `send2trash` refuses to send a recycle-bin file back to the recycle bin (`WinError -2147024809`, the user-reported cluster behind #482). `#recycle` — Synology DSM's per-share Recycle Bin, which sits at the root of every share with the recycle bin enabled — was added in [#821](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/821); before that a source pointed at a NAS share root walked straight into the user's deleted files.
- **Conditions / variants:** The rule is an **ancestor** predicate bounded by the scan root, so (a) a file merely *named* like a skip folder (`#recycle.jpg`) and a folder whose name only *contains* one (`$RECYCLE.BIN backup 2019`) are scanned normally, and (b) a source deliberately pointed AT a recycle bin still walks its contents — the root is the user's declared scope. The same function is the single definition used by the manifest-open reconcile (see [Open Manifest — base flow](#open-manifest--base-flow)); the folder names live in exactly one place.
- **Related:** [#482](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/482) (Windows/macOS names), [#821](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/821) (Synology `#recycle` + the stale-row reconcile). Pinned by `tests/test_walker.py::TestWalkerSkipDirectories` and `::TestHasSkipDirectoryAncestor`; QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s09_walker_exclusions.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s09_walker_exclusions.py) covers the sibling `SKIP_FILENAMES` / `MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` guards.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — checked against the shipped web client; no change); 2026-09-03 (#821 — `#recycle` added; previously undocumented, entry written with the change)

### Scan flow — rescan confirm

- **Entry point:** `RescanConfirmDialog` ([frontend/src/components/dialogs/RescanConfirmDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/dialogs/RescanConfirmDialog.tsx)), a nested modal portaled over the still-open Scan dialog ([frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx)). Wrapper testid `scan-rescan-confirm-dialog`.
- **Trigger:** User clicks **Start Scan** while the loaded manifest has pending decisions. `ScanDialog` counts every loaded-manifest row whose `user_decision` is non-empty (**Delete** or **Skip** — **Keep** is the default `""` and never counts) and gates **Start Scan** behind the dialog only when that count is `> 0`; with zero staged decisions the scan starts immediately, no confirm.
- **Behaviour:** Title **Discard pending decisions?**; the body interpolates the pluralised count ("You have N pending decision(s) on the loaded manifest. Starting a new scan will discard them."). The count and its noun are two separate placeholders in the template, so each locale joins them its own way (zh_TW takes no space before a measure word; copy audit RC1). **Cancel** (testid `scan-rescan-confirm-cancel`; Escape and clicking outside do the same) keeps the loaded manifest and leaves the Scan dialog open. **Discard & Rescan** (testid `scan-rescan-confirm-discard`) starts the scan, which builds the new manifest from scratch and loads it when it finishes.
- **Conditions / variants:** What is actually lost depends on the output path. If the scan's output path is the loaded manifest's path, the finished scan atomically replaces that file and the pending decisions are gone from disk; otherwise the previous manifest stays on disk untouched but is no longer the one on screen. Either way nothing is replaced until the new scan finishes: a scan that is cancelled or fails leaves the existing file as it was.
- **Related:** [#142](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/142); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s27_rescan_confirm.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s27_rescan_confirm.py) (both Cancel-keeps-manifest and Discard-rebuilds-manifest branches, asserted via `GET /api/manifest`); `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.test.tsx` (the gate opens only with pending decisions; Discard & Rescan proceeds; Cancel keeps the dialog open); `tests/test_scan_runner.py::TestAutoSelectPipeline::test_failed_rescan_preserves_prior_manifest` and `tests/test_scan_pipeline.py::TestPipelineLateCancel::test_cancel_before_write_only_preserves_existing_manifest` (the old file survives a scan that doesn't finish).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-07-22 (web-docs-drift backfill — `RescanConfirmDialog` documented)

### Scan flow — visual selection of KEEP rows after scan

- **Entry point:** The post-scan manifest load in [frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.tsx) → `loadManifest(path, { selectKeepers })` in [frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts](../frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts) → the one-shot keeper scroll in [frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx).
- **Trigger:** A scan run with **Auto select after scan** on finishes. The dialog loads the new manifest automatically and closes — there is no separate Close & Load step.
- **Behaviour:** Instead of clearing the selection, as every other manifest load does, this load selects every row whose `action` is `KEEP` — the auto-select keepers — so they render highlighted (`aria-selected="true"`) in the result tree; it scrolls the first keeper to the centre of the tree and puts the keyboard cursor on it, so the next arrow key continues from there. The user sees at a glance which keepers the scorer picked — eliminating the "what just happened?" moment after a silent auto-select. The keepers also carry their lock and **Keep** decision (see [Scan dialog — auto-select after scan](#scan-dialog--auto-select-after-scan)).
- **Conditions / variants:** Only the post-scan load of a scan that ran with auto-select on does this. The checkbox is frozen while the scan runs, so the value used is the one the scan ran with; without auto-select there are no `KEEP` rows and the load clears the selection as usual. Opening the same manifest later (Open Manifest) does not re-select the keepers. The scroll is one-shot: if the first keeper's group is collapsed the signal is dropped, and a later plain click never re-positions the viewport. The keeper selection is an ordinary multi-selection (see [Web — main result tree multi-selection](#web--main-result-tree-multi-selection)).
- **Related:** [PR #255](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/255) (fix for [#239](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/239)); web port [#692](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/692). Pinned by `frontend/src/store/useAppStore.test.ts` ("loadManifest – selectKeepers (#692)"), `frontend/src/components/ResultTree.test.tsx` ("highlights the keeper and consumes the one-shot scroll signal (#692)") and `frontend/src/components/ScanDialog.test.tsx` ("passes selectKeepers:true to loadManifest when auto-select is enabled (#692)"); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s49_scan_auto_select.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s49_scan_auto_select.py) (the keeper row is `aria-selected="true"`, a non-keeper is not).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Scan grouping — complementary RAW + lossy pair

- **Entry point:** The result tree after a scan — a RAW original and its lossy export now appear as **one review group** instead of two files the tree never shows. Classifier: [scanner/dedup.py](../scanner/dedup.py) `_classify_format_group` / `_complementary_edges`.
- **Trigger:** A scan finds two or more files with an **identical** pHash where at least one is RAW (`.dng` / `.cr2` / `.cr3` / `.nef` / `.arw` / `.raf` / `.rw2` / `.tif` / `.tiff` — the extensions [scanner/media.py](../scanner/media.py) `get_file_type` maps to `raw`) — i.e. a RAW plus its JPEG/HEIC export, or a CR2 plus its DNG conversion. No user action.
- **Behaviour:** The bucket forms one group (shared `group_id`) so the files are shown side by side. **No dedup decision is taken:** every member keeps `action = ""` (undecided / "Ref" tier), because a RAW and its lossy export are complementary — the user keeps both unless they decide otherwise. Aggressive auto-select ("delete all others") still cannot touch them: only rows the classifier positively marked `EXACT` / `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` are delete-eligible ([Scan dialog — auto-select aggressive ("delete all others")](#scan-dialog--auto-select-aggressive-delete-all-others)). Non-aggressive auto-select does lock the higher-scoring member as the group keeper, exactly as it does for any other group.
- **A RAW never causes its lossy export to be auto-deleted.** Because a RAW usually outscores its JPEG, joining a group can make the RAW the keeper — and the lossy row it displaced would otherwise lose the only protection it had (being the keeper) and fall into the auto-delete set if it happened to carry `EXACT`. So when a group's keeper is a RAW, the **best non-RAW row in that group is protected alongside it**, in both the scan-time aggressive path and the review-time [Apply best-copy](#web--context-menu-apply-best-copy-decisions-to-this-group) action ([core/services/auto_select.py](../core/services/auto_select.py) `_raw_displaced_keepers`). Duplicates ranked *below* that row are still deleted — a RAW in the group does not switch auto-delete off wholesale.
- **Conditions / variants:** Only applies to an **exact** pHash match; a near-duplicate RAW/JPEG pair (Hamming 1-threshold) was already grouped by the near-duplicate tier. The pair is dropped — no group — when the two mean colours differ beyond the threshold ([#462](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/462)) or when dHash positively disagrees ([#524](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/524)): those are pHash false positives, not one shot. Same-stem RAW+JPG pairs in one folder were already grouped by the filename-cluster edge ([#88](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/88)); what changes here is the pair whose names or folders **differ** (a renamed or relocated export). **Existing manifests are unaffected until a rescan** — `group_id` is written at scan time and `ManifestRepository.rescore` does not recompute grouping. Measured on the author's 40,774-row library: 200 exact RAW+lossy pairs, of which 79 gain a shared `group_id`; 125 files that were invisible singletons enter review, and 442 of 8,304 existing groups get a different `group_id` because merged components take a new lexicographically-smallest anchor. **Read the 79 as base-code-vs-head-code, not as a count of what is wrong in the library today:** both figures come from re-running `classify()` over a copy of that manifest, and the reconstruction has no stored `dhash` / `mean_color`, so its grouping is slightly more permissive than the scan that wrote the file. The manifest's own stored grouping leaves only **15** of the 200 pairs ungrouped — that column is the residue of an older scan, not a base measurement.
- **Related:** [#824](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/824); prior art [#88](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/88) (group together, decide independently) and [#538](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/538) (edges decoupled from classification); rendering covered by [Similarity column](#similarity-column) (the pair renders as Ref + `N*%` passenger); unit tests `tests/test_dedup.py::TestComplementaryGroupEdge`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-04 (#824)

### Set Action dialog — dual-section Simple + Regex view

- **Entry point:** The string-field body of the Set Action dialog: [frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx) (Simple row + Regex row), mounted by [frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx) whenever the selected field is not numeric. The rest of the dialog (how to open it, the action combo, Recent, the delete-confirm and lock-confirm flows) is described in [Web — Action dialog (Set Action by Field)](#web--action-dialog-set-action-by-field).
- **Trigger:** The dialog is open with a string field selected: File Name (the default), Folder, Resolution, Action or Lock. Both rows are always shown together. There is no mode toggle.
- **Behaviour:**
  - **Simple row** (`action-simple-op` + `action-simple-text`) reads "Find rows where it [contains | starts with | ends with | exactly matches] [text]". Every edit writes through to the Regex input. The text is regex-escaped, then left bare / prefixed `^` / suffixed `$` / wrapped `^…$` for the four operators, so plain text containing `.`, `(` or `+` stays literal.
  - **Regex row** (`action-regex-input`) holds the pattern that is actually sent. It is the single source of truth. A ○ / ✓ / ✗ icon sits beside it (see [Set Action dialog — live preview + validation](#set-action-dialog--live-preview--validation)). Seven cheatsheet chips (`action-cheatsheet-*`: `.*`, `\d`, `\w`, `^`, `$`, `\.`, `[abc]`) insert their token at the caret, replacing any selection, and leave the caret after it.
  - **The sync runs one way, Simple → Regex.** The Simple row is derived from the pattern only when the panel mounts, which happens on open and on every field change (the panel is keyed on the field). It is derived by reverse-parsing the pattern (`reverseParseSimple` in [frontend/src/lib/patternSummary.ts](../frontend/src/lib/patternSummary.ts)). A pattern that Simple cannot express leaves the row at "contains" + blank. Typing in the Regex row, inserting a chip or picking a Recent entry does not update the Simple row.
  - Matching runs on the server (`POST /api/action/bulk-decide`, [core/app_service/action_resolve.py](../core/app_service/action_resolve.py)) with Python `re`, **case-insensitive**. Each field is matched against: File Name = the basename; Folder = the row's folder path; Action = the stored decision (`""` for Keep / no decision, `delete`, `ignore`); Lock = `Locked` or `""`; Resolution = `W×H`. See [Set Action dialog — Score / Lock / Resolution fields](#set-action-dialog--score--lock--resolution-fields).
- **Conditions / variants:**
  - **Empty values:** a blank Simple text always produces an empty pattern, whichever operator is selected, so "exactly matches" + blank does **not** produce `^$`. To select rows whose value is empty, type `^$` into the Regex row (for example Lock `^$` = unlocked rows, Action `^$` = rows with no decision). An empty pattern matches nothing (the server returns no rows for it), so Apply with an empty pattern is a no-op.
  - **No groups in the review:** when the loaded review has zero groups, the Simple operator and text are disabled and an italic note reads "Write-through preview unavailable — Simple inputs are read-only on this entry point." The Regex row stays usable ([#689](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/689), s55).
  - **Starting state:** every open resets the dialog to field "File Name" (unless the entry point pre-fills a field from the clicked column, [#735](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/735)), Simple operator "contains" and action **Delete**. None of the three is remembered between opens. Opening from a row seeds the pattern with that row's escaped value ([#893](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/893)), and the Simple row then shows it as `contains <value>`. Which entry point seeds from which row is listed under [Web — Action dialog (Set Action by Field)](#web--action-dialog-set-action-by-field).
  - Picking a numeric field replaces both rows with the numeric panel ([Set Action dialog — numeric comparison panel](#set-action-dialog--numeric-comparison-panel)).
  - **Keyboard and dismissal:** there is no Apply shortcut, and there are no Alt mnemonics or inline clear buttons (the retired desktop dialog's Ctrl+Enter, Alt-letter mnemonics and × buttons were not ported). Escape closes the dialog unless an apply is in flight. A click outside the dialog never closes it ([#764](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/764)). The dialog is always a modal overlay.
  - A successful Apply closes the dialog and the result tree re-renders from the server's post-apply groups. The retired desktop dialog stayed open and showed "Applied to N rows" instead.
- **Related:** [frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx); `frontend/src/lib/regexEscape.ts`; `frontend/src/lib/patternSummary.ts` (`reverseParseSimple`); `core/app_service/action_resolve.py` (`resolve_matched_paths`, `_get_record_field`); component tests [`frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx) (write-through for all four operators, the zero-groups branch, #893 seeding); unit tests `tests/test_action_resolve.py`; QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s31_simple_mode_regex.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s31_simple_mode_regex.py) (Simple write-through → preview → Apply), [`qa/web/scenarios/s55_action_dialog_no_match_fn.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s55_action_dialog_no_match_fn.py) (zero-groups branch), [`qa/web/scenarios/s73_action_seed_and_page_scroll.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s73_action_seed_and_page_scroll.py) (#893 row seed). Design history: [PR #167](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/167), [PR #168](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/168), [#396](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/396) (the dual-section view replaced the Simple/Regex mode toggle).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Set Action dialog — live preview + validation

- **Entry point:** The match counter, error line and Preview list in [frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx); the regex check in [frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/RegexPanel.tsx); the dry run of `POST /api/action/bulk-decide` with `preview: true` ([app/web/routes/action.py](../app/web/routes/action.py) → `bulk_decide` in [core/app_service/action_service.py](../core/app_service/action_service.py)).
- **Trigger:** Any change of field or pattern: the Simple row, the Regex row, a cheatsheet chip, a Recent pick, or a numeric-panel input. The request fires 300 ms after the last change. Nothing is requested while the pattern is empty. Changing only the action combo does not re-run it.
- **Behaviour:**
  - The server resolves the pattern against the manifest exactly as a real Apply would, writes nothing, and returns the match count and paths. The store drops out-of-order responses, so the counter never shows a superseded pattern's result.
  - **Match counter** (`action-match-counter`) shows "N of M matched", where M is the number of files in the loaded review. For a Top-N pattern it shows "N matched (≤n per group × G groups)". It shows "—" while the pattern is empty or the answer has not arrived or failed, and "updating…" appears beside it while a request is in flight.
  - **Preview list** (`action-preview-list`) shows the full path of every matched row, in group order, in a scrollable box. "No matches" appears when the count is 0. The server does not cap the list today, so the "…and N more" footer (`action-preview-truncated`) never appears in practice.
  - The preview **counts locked rows too**. The locked-rows gate runs only on a real Apply (the lock-confirm flow is described under [Web — Action dialog (Set Action by Field)](#web--action-dialog-set-action-by-field)).
  - **Validation:** the ○ / ✓ / ✗ icon updates on every keystroke. For an invalid pattern, a red "Invalid regex: <message>" line (`action-validation-error`) appears under the Regex row. That check uses the browser's JavaScript `RegExp`, while matching uses the server's Python `re`. When the server rejects a pattern (HTTP 400 `invalid_pattern`), its response shows in the dialog's red alert line instead of a preview. The two grammars differ at the edges (e.g. `(?P<name>…)` is Python-only), so the server's answer is the authoritative one. The numeric panel has its own input check ([Set Action dialog — numeric comparison panel](#set-action-dialog--numeric-comparison-panel)).
  - **Apply is never disabled by the pattern.** It is disabled only while a request is running or when no manifest is loaded ([#397](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/397), restored on the web by [#741](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/741)). An empty pattern applies to nothing.
- **Conditions / variants:** The same dialog, with the same preview, opens from the toolbar, the menu bar, a result-tree row's right-click, and the Execute Action dialog's row menu ([#316](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/316)). The Execute route opens it manifest-wide, with no row seed. A successful Apply closes the dialog and the tree re-renders from the server's post-apply groups; the web shows no separate status-bar notice for it.
- **Related:** `app/web/routes/action.py` (400 `invalid_pattern`, 409 `locked_paths`, 422); `core/app_service/action_service.py` (`bulk_decide`, `preview`); `core/app_service/action_resolve.py`; `frontend/src/store/useAppStore.ts` (`previewBulkDecide`, stale-response sequence); unit tests `tests/test_web_action_routes.py`, `tests/test_action_resolve.py`, [`frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx) (match counter, Apply enabled on an empty pattern), `frontend/src/store/useAppStore.test.ts` (out-of-order preview dropped); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s14_action_by_regex.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s14_action_by_regex.py) (menu-bar route, live counter, Apply), [`qa/web/scenarios/s30_execute_dialog_regex_right_click.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s30_execute_dialog_regex_right_click.py) (Execute-dialog row menu → same dialog). History: [PR #162](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/162) (live preview, validation, right-click parity); [#395](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/395) removed the desktop "Test against:" playground.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Set Action dialog — numeric comparison panel

- **Entry point:** [frontend/src/components/action/NumericPanel.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/NumericPanel.tsx) (`action-numeric-row`). It replaces the Simple + Regex rows when the field is numeric. The server resolves it with `resolve_matched_paths` → `select_paths_by_threshold` / `select_paths_top_n` in [core/app_service/action_resolve.py](../core/app_service/action_resolve.py).
- **Trigger:** Picking one of the six numeric fields: Size (Bytes), Group Count, Similarity, Score, Creation Date or Shot Date. One dialog component serves every entry point (toolbar, menu bar, row right-click, Execute dialog row menu), so every route reaches the panel.
- **Behaviour:**
  - The panel has two radio modes:
    - **Threshold** is the default: "Where the value is [> | >= | < | <= | == | !=] [value]". The operator defaults to `>=`, and the input encodes as `__cmp__:OP:VALUE`.
    - **Top N per group:** "Pick the [Top | Bottom] [N] in each group". It defaults to Top, 3, and encodes as `__top_n__:N:desc` (Top) or `__top_n__:N:asc` (Bottom). Switching to Top N sends that default pattern immediately, so a preview appears without typing.
  - The pseudo-pattern takes the same `POST /api/action/bulk-decide` path, for preview and for Apply, as a regex does. A `__cmp__:` / `__top_n__:` prefix commits the server to that branch: a malformed payload is rejected (HTTP 422) and is never re-read as a regex.
  - **Threshold:** rows with no value for the field are skipped, and dates are compared as timestamps.
  - **Top N:** within each group, rows are ranked by the field, with ties broken by file path, so the same configuration always selects the same rows. Rows with no value are left out of the ranking. A group with fewer than N rankable rows contributes all of them.
  - The ranking is `core.services.auto_select.top_n_paths` ([#778](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/778)), the same rule behind the per-group "Apply best-copy decisions to this group" row-menu item and the "Keep best · delete rest" group-row button ([#744](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/744); see [Web — Context menu: Apply best-copy decisions to this group](#web--context-menu-apply-best-copy-decisions-to-this-group)). A Top-N Apply sets the chosen action on the selected rows only, while those two also decide the rest of the group.
  - **What each field compares:**
    - **Size (Bytes):** bytes. The result tree shows a human-readable size.
    - **Group Count:** the number of files in the row's group.
    - **Similarity:** the row's stored pHash Hamming distance (0 = identical, larger = less similar). This is not the percentage the Similarity badge shows.
    - **Score:** the 0–1 value the Score column shows.
    - **Creation Date / Shot Date:** the stored date-time, compared as a timestamp. A typed `YYYY-MM-DD` means local midnight of that day, so `==` matches only rows stamped exactly 00:00:00; bracket a day with `>=` and `<` instead.
- **Conditions / variants:**
  - **Threshold input:** the placeholder reads "type a date (YYYY-MM-DD)…" for the two date fields and "type a number…" otherwise. Input that fails the check (a date not shaped `YYYY-MM-DD`, or a non-number) shows "Couldn't parse '<input>' as a value for this field" (`action-numeric-error`) and clears the pattern. The counter then shows "—" and nothing is previewed. The date check tests the shape only: `2026-13-45` passes it and matches nothing. There is no ✓ / ✗ icon in this mode.
  - **Top N:** the counter reads "N matched (≤n per group × G groups)". The N field is a number input bounded 1–10,000 (the stepper stops there; a typed value is checked only to be ≥ 1). Preview rows are plain file paths, as for a regex.
  - **Switching fields keeps the encoded pattern.** The panel remounts on a field change and renders blank inputs, but the store keeps the last pattern unless it was the previous field's row seed or empty, and numeric fields have no seed. A `__cmp__:` / `__top_n__:` pattern built for one field therefore survives a switch to another numeric field, and preview and Apply run it against the new field — e.g. a Score `>= 0.5` becomes Shot Date `>= 0.5`, read as a timestamp. Re-enter the condition after changing the field.
  - Numeric patterns are never recorded in Recent.
- **Related:** [PR #221](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/221) (closes [#209](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/209)); `core/app_service/action_resolve.py`; `core/services/auto_select.py` (`top_n_paths`); unit tests `tests/test_action_resolve.py`, `tests/test_topn_keeper_parity.py`, [`frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.test.tsx) (`__cmp__` / `__top_n__` encoding). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s43_numeric_condition.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s43_numeric_condition.py) (Size threshold → Apply → exact 3-of-5 split), [`qa/web/scenarios/s50_select_numeric_panel_from_main_window.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s50_select_numeric_panel_from_main_window.py) (menu-bar route reaches the panel), [`qa/web/scenarios/s56_action_dialog_apply_by_score.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s56_action_dialog_apply_by_score.py) (Score threshold writes rows — the [#392](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/392) regression).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Set Action dialog — Score / Lock / Resolution fields

- **Entry point:** The Field combo (`action-field-combo`) in [frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx). The field → record-attribute mapping lives in [core/app_service/action_resolve.py](../core/app_service/action_resolve.py) (`_FIELD_TO_ATTR`, `_get_record_field`, `_numeric_value_for`).
- **Trigger:** User opens the dialog from any entry point and expands the Field combo.
- **Behaviour:** Every entry point shows the same field list, in this order, because one mounted `ActionDialog` serves them all: File Name, Folder, Size (Bytes), Group Count, Similarity, Score, Creation Date, Shot Date, Resolution, Action, Lock. The six numeric fields open the numeric panel ([Set Action dialog — numeric comparison panel](#set-action-dialog--numeric-comparison-panel)). The other five take a regex ([Set Action dialog — dual-section Simple + Regex view](#set-action-dialog--dual-section-simple--regex-view)).
  - **Score** is numeric: the 0–1 value the Score column shows.
  - **Lock** matches the string `Locked` for a locked row and `""` for an unlocked one: use `^Locked$` / `^$`.
  - **Resolution** matches `W×H`, with the `×` sign and no spaces (e.g. `^1920×1080$`), which is the same text the app displays for dimensions. Rows without both dimensions (typically videos) never match.
  - **Action** matches the stored decision: `""` (Keep / no decision), `delete`, or `ignore` (Skip).
- **Conditions / variants:** Labels are translated through the `web.column.*` keys. The option value stays the canonical English label, which is what the server receives. The Execute Action dialog's row menu opens this same component, so its field list is identical by construction. The retired desktop client once had a shorter hard-coded list on that route ([#392](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/392)). The same 11 labels are duplicated in `frontend/src/lib/actionDialogFields.ts` (`ACTION_DIALOG_FIELD_OPTIONS`, which validates a pre-filled field) and are kept in step with the combo's list by hand.
- **Related:** [PR #250](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/250) (closes [#238](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/238)); `core/app_service/action_resolve.py`; `frontend/src/lib/actionDialogFields.ts`; `frontend/src/lib/format.ts` (`formatDims`); unit tests `tests/test_action_resolve.py`, [`frontend/src/lib/actionDialogFields.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/actionDialogFields.test.ts); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s50_select_numeric_panel_from_main_window.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s50_select_numeric_panel_from_main_window.py) (Score opens the numeric panel), [`qa/web/scenarios/s56_action_dialog_apply_by_score.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s56_action_dialog_apply_by_score.py) (Score threshold Apply).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Set Action dialog — geometry persistence

- **Entry point:** The shared overlay-geometry mechanism: `useOverlayGeometry("action", …)` in [frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx](../frontend/src/components/action/ActionDialog.tsx), the persistence helpers in [frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.ts](../frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.ts), the title bar `action-title-bar` and the corner grip `action-resize-handle`. The full behaviour is described in [Web — overlay window geometry (movable / resizable / persisted)](#web--overlay-window-geometry-movable--resizable--persisted).
- **Trigger:** User drags the dialog's title bar (move) or its bottom-right grip (resize).
- **Behaviour:** The position, plus the size after a resize, is saved to the browser's localStorage under `pm.overlay-geometry.action.v1`, once per gesture. It is restored the next time the dialog opens, including after a page reload. A move stores position only, so the dialog still grows when its Preview list appears. A resize pins width and height; the body (`action-body`) then scrolls while the Cancel / Apply footer stays in view. Saved geometry is clamped into the current viewport, with a 320×280 px minimum.
- **Conditions / variants:** On first open (nothing saved, or storage unreadable) the dialog uses its default centered `max-w-2xl` layout. The geometry is stored per browser profile, not in `settings.json`. The web has **no Reset window size button or shortcut**; the retired desktop dialog's Reset button and Ctrl+0 have no counterpart. Clearing the `pm.overlay-geometry.action.v1` key (browser site data) returns the dialog to its default. The dialog is a single column with the preview under the inputs, so there is no splitter position to persist.
- **Related:** issue [#739](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/739) (divergence D7 in [`docs/audits/web-divergence-list-2026-07.md`](audits/web-divergence-list-2026-07.md)); QA scenario [`qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s48_dialog_geometry_persist.py) (move keeps auto-height, resize to the floor scrolls the body, geometry restored after reload); unit tests [`frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/overlayGeometry.test.ts), [`frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/hooks/useOverlayGeometry.test.tsx). Desktop history: [PR #228](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/228) (closes [#215](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/215)), [#391](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/391).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-05 (#739 — live-run via s48, per the overlay-geometry entry)
