### Web — filesystem browse dialog

- **Entry point:** `FsBrowser` ([frontend/src/components/FsBrowser.tsx](../frontend/src/components/FsBrowser.tsx)), backed by `GET /api/fs/browse` ([app/web/routes/fs.py:12](../app/web/routes/fs.py#L12) → `core/app_service/fs_browse.py` `browse()`). The web's only filesystem picker — there is no native OS file dialog on the web port.
- **Trigger:** Three flows mount it in three different `mode`s:
  - **`mode="file"`** — Open Manifest, from the menu bar **File > Open Manifest…** and the empty-state **Open Manifest…** button ([frontend/src/App.tsx:486-493](../frontend/src/App.tsx#L486)); see [Open Manifest — base flow](#open-manifest--base-flow) § Web parity.
  - **`mode="directory"`** — each Scan-dialog source row's **Browse** button (picks a folder to add as a scan source).
  - **`mode="save"`** — the Scan dialog's output-path **Browse** button (picks a folder + types a filename for the output `.db`) — this is also the "in-DOM save-mode picker" already referenced from [Web — scan dialog invalid source path](#web--scan-dialog-invalid-source-path).
- **Behaviour:** Opens at `initialPath` (falling back to its parent directory if that can't be listed, then to the filesystem roots — never dead-ends on an error). The roots view (testid `fs-browser-path` shows "This PC") lists Windows drive letters A-Z that exist, or a single `/` on POSIX. Navigation: clicking a directory entry (testid `fs-browser-entry`, `data-kind="dir"`) descends into it; **Up** (testid `fs-browser-up`, disabled at the roots) returns to `parent`. `directory` mode filters the listing to folders only; `file`/`save` modes also show files, with `.sqlite`/`.db` entries tagged `is_manifest` (a "manifest" pill, case-insensitive on both extensions). Confirm target varies by mode: `directory` → the current folder (disabled at the roots); `file` → the single selected file (double-clicking a file entry confirms immediately); `save` → `<cwd>/<filename>` from the filename input (testid `fs-browser-filename`, disabled until non-blank). **Confirm**/**Cancel** buttons are testids `fs-browser-confirm`/`fs-browser-cancel`. A listing error (e.g. the path was removed between opens) renders inline via testid `fs-browser-error` (`role="alert"`) rather than closing the dialog.
- **Conditions / variants:** **No root restriction (user-visible):** `GET /api/fs/browse` does **not** call the shared `_path_guard.validate_under_roots` helper that gates [`execute`](#execute-action--base-flow), [`image`](#preview-pane--byte-budget-lru-cache), and [`media`](#web-preview-pane--video-playback-v1-streaming) — `core/app_service/fs_browse.py`'s `browse()` takes any path and lists it (404 if it doesn't exist, 400 if it's not a directory). Concretely: the picker can navigate anywhere on the local filesystem the server process can read, not just the app's configured source/output roots — this is a deliberate scope difference from the mutating/data-serving routes (a directory listing is lower-risk than a file read or a delete), but it IS the kind of filesystem-information-disclosure surface [Web — scan dialog invalid source path](#web--scan-dialog-invalid-source-path) explicitly declined to add elsewhere (`GET /api/fs/stat`) — this endpoint already provides an even broader version of that disclosure, just for directory listings rather than single-path existence checks. The exemption is a recorded decision (2026-07-22) in [fs.py](../app/web/routes/fs.py)'s module docstring: acceptable while the server binds loopback-only — revisit before any LAN/proxy exposure or privileged execution. A rapid double-click or React StrictMode double-invoke is protected by a monotonic request-sequence guard so a stale response can never clobber a newer directory's listing.
- **Related:** [app/web/routes/fs.py](../app/web/routes/fs.py); [core/app_service/fs_browse.py](../core/app_service/fs_browse.py); [app/web/routes/_path_guard.py](../app/web/routes/_path_guard.py) (the guard this endpoint does NOT use); consuming flows: [Open Manifest — base flow](#open-manifest--base-flow) § Web parity, [Web — ScanDialog source/output persistence](#web--scandialog-sourceoutput-persistence), [Web — scan dialog invalid source path](#web--scan-dialog-invalid-source-path); qa/web scenarios [`s16_open_manifest.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s16_open_manifest.py) (file mode via `open_manifest_via_picker`), [`s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s17_scan_dialog_widgets.py) (directory/save modes referenced from the Scan dialog).
- **Last verified:** 2026-07-22 (web-docs-drift backfill — initial documentation of this surface; no prior features.md entry existed)

### Web — Daylight theme and results-tree framing

- **Entry point:** [`frontend/src/index.css`](../frontend/src/index.css) — one `@theme` block holding every Daylight colour token; consumed as ordinary Tailwind utilities (`bg-app`, `border-hairline`, `text-ink`, `bg-group-band`, `bg-delete-row`, the `sim-*` badge trios) by [`App.tsx`](../frontend/src/App.tsx), [`MenuBar.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/MenuBar.tsx), [`ResultTree.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx), the `components/result/` rows, and — since slice (e) — every remaining surface: the preview pane, both context menus, every dialog, and the shared `components/ui/*` primitives (`button`, `checkbox`, `dialog`, `select`, `progress`).
- **Trigger:** Always on — there is no theme picker. The light "Daylight" palette is the web client's only theme.
- **Behaviour:** The app canvas is warm paper (`#f5efe6`) with near-white panels (`#fffdf9`), hairline warm-gray borders, warm-terracotta accent (`#a85a2c`) and themed scrollbar thumbs, replacing the stock Tailwind neutral palette the port shipped with. In the results tree: a **group header** carries a warm band (`#f3e9d8`), a top border and a 4 px accent left strip, and the frame is **closed by a border under the group's last child** — carried as row data (`isLastInGroup`, set while flattening `vrows`) rather than a CSS `:last-child` rule, because the tree is virtualised and the DOM's last child is whatever the virtualizer happened to mount. A row staged for deletion (`user_decision === "delete"`) gets a soft red wash (`#fdf3f0`) and a struck-through filename; selection is a warm tint (`#f1e3d0`) instead of the browser-default blue — a **tint only**, since layout slice T: it carried a 1px terracotta inset ring as well, which sat a pixel inside the keyboard cursor's 2px accent outline in the same hue, so on a multi-selection the ▸ caret was the only thing still saying where the cursor was. Fill = state, stroke = cursor, one accent stroke on the screen; the tree container's own focus cue is a hairline ring for the same reason. The keeper's 2px left strip is the accent token too (`#a85a2c`, was the Ref badge's own `#a85f2e` — 5/255 from the group strip directly above it in one vertical line, which read as a rendering fault rather than two cues), and hover is the subtle warm tint. Row selection, decisions, locking, the keyboard cursor, the column model and the sticky header are unchanged.
  - **Decision chips (slice b).** The three-segment Keep / Delete / Skip control ([`DecisionControl.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/result/DecisionControl.tsx)) keeps its segments, labels, testids and one-click behaviour, and is retoned: the **active** segment wears its own decision's chip — `Keep` green (`#e7f3ec` on `#2f8a5a`, because `''` IS keep under the [#584](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/584) model), `Delete` filled red (`#c4503f` / white), `Skip` warm grey (`#f1ece2`) — and every inactive segment is transparent with faint ink, so exactly one filled chip is visible per row. Segment geometry is unchanged, so no cell width moves.
  - **Padlock (slice b).** The lock control ([`LockToggle.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/result/LockToggle.tsx)) is a padlock button instead of a checkbox: **open shackle, right leg lifted, in `ink-muted` `#6b6358`** when unlocked, **closed shackle in warm `#a85a2c`** when locked (both glyphs draw the same centred shackle arc), reporting `aria-pressed` (plus the `data-state="checked"/"unchecked"` the checkbox emitted). Clicking dispatches the same lock action and, as before, also selects the row. The sticky column header gains a matching **padlock glyph** over that column, with the translated "Lock" as its accessible name. The unlocked ink was the hairline `#a89f8f` until design ruling F1 ([#922](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/922)): the padlock answers "is this row protected from the delete verb?", which is a state, so it moved to `ink-muted` (5.8:1 on panel, 4.7:1 on the selection tint, against 2.58:1 before) and `hairline` now carries no state anywhere. Because `ink-muted` and warm are near-identical in grayscale, the open-vs-closed shackle is what separates the two states.
  - **Score mini bar (slice b, re-specified by slice R).** The Score cell renders the formatted number with a warm bar on a `#ece4d6` track beneath it whose width is the score's fraction of 1. Slice R made the fill **solid `#b8946a`** and deleted the second gradient stop (`--color-score-fill-end`) — at a 96px cell a two-stop gradient is three or four distinguishable pixels of variation, invisible at best and a banding artefact at worst. The width rule is a pure function ([`frontend/src/lib/scoreBar.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/scoreBar.ts)) porting the Qt delegate's `max(0, min(1, score))` clamp; an **unscored** row (`score === null`, e.g. a Live Photo MOV passenger) shows the em dash and **no track at all**, so "unscored" never reads as "scored zero". The bar contributes no text; slice R added a `keep` LABEL above the number, so the number's exact handle is the `data-score-value` element rather than the cell's whole text.
  - **The rest of the screen (slice e).** Colour only — no label, layout, size, radius, testid, handler or keyboard behaviour moves. The **preview pane** and its metadata strip are the panel surface (`#fffdf9`) with hairline borders and `ink` / `ink-muted` text; the **status bar** strip is the titlebar surface (`#f3ede3`); both **context menus** (row and execute-tree) are panel with `#f7ebda` hover fills and hairline separators; **every dialog** (Scan, Settings, Action + its regex/numeric panels, Execute, the lock / prune confirms, the filesystem browser) inherits the panel surface from `ui/dialog.tsx`, with `#ddd2bf` input borders and a terracotta focus ring in place of the neutral one. Primary buttons and a ticked checkbox fill with the accent (`#a85a2c`, hover `#8f4b23`, active `#7a3f1d`) instead of near-black; destructive buttons and error text use the warm danger red (`#c4503f`); the execute tree's group strip uses the warm band and its per-row Delete / Skip pills reuse the decision chips. A modal's backdrop is the ink at the same opacity it always had.
  - **Type, ink ramp, accent and the caution role (layout slice T).** Tokens and two component touches; no geometry, no testid, no behaviour moves. The app declares a **font family** for the first time — `--font-sans` `'Segoe UI', system-ui, -apple-system, 'Noto Sans TC', sans-serif` (applied on `body`; `Noto Sans TC` sits ahead of the generic so a zh-TW label does not fall back per glyph) and `--font-mono` `'Cascadia Code', ui-monospace, Menlo, monospace` (the `font-mono` utility, worn today by the preview pane's metadata values and the Action dialog's pattern fields). The **accent** moves `#bd6b39` → **`#a85a2c`** in one token, so the group strip, the ★ Ref border, the primary button and the focus ring stay one colour; **hover and active now go darker** (`#8f4b23`, `#7a3f1d`) rather than lighter, which is why the old hover measured worse than rest. The **ink ramp** becomes three tiers: `ink` `#2c2823`, `ink-muted` **`#6b6358`** (was `#867d70`) and `ink-faint` `#a89f8f`, whose VALUE is unchanged but whose ROLE narrows to **non-text** — rules, dividers, disabled glyph strokes and placeholders. Every place `#a89f8f` was carrying text a human reads for meaning (folder sub-lines, empty-state copy, menu section labels, dialog hints) moves to `ink-muted` (so, later, does the unlocked padlock glyph — design F1, #922); the group header's `·` separator, disabled input text, the dimmed non-selectable rows in the filesystem browser and the select placeholder stay. A **caution role** is declared — `caution-ink` `#6b4e12` on `caution-bg` `#fdf3d9` with a `#e3c264` border and the ▲ glyph, pulled toward yellow-olive so it cannot be misread as the terracotta accent; it is declared here so the banners and chips that follow have one definition to share, and **layout slice E is its first consumer** (the execute dialog's two warning banners, below).
  - **Keyboard-cursor focus ring (layout slice T).** See [Main window — keyboard navigation](#main-window--keyboard-navigation) — the row the roving cursor names now draws a visible ring.
  - **The row box, its controls and the two densities (layout slice R).** The file row gets the design reply's absolute geometry, specified twice — once **comfortable** (the default), once **compact** — and keyed on a new persisted preference. Every number lives in one table, [`frontend/src/lib/rowMetrics.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/rowMetrics.ts), because the virtualiser's `estimateSize` and the row's own classes have to agree or arrow-key scroll-into-view lands a row under the sticky header. **Row box:** total height **72px / 52px** *including* the 1px bottom rule, 11/7px vertical and 16/14px horizontal padding, 12/10px gap, and — the single biggest change — the cells are **vertically centred**, not top-aligned: six short cells floating at the top of a tall box against a 48px thumbnail is what made the row read as a spreadsheet. A group's **last child is 6/4px taller** (78 / 56) with the closing rule painted under that pad, so a group ends with a breath instead of a butt-joint; that is a second number in `estimateSize`, and it reads the `isLast` flag the group frame already carries. **Thumbnail:** 48/36px, radius 8/6, a 1px hairline border, `object-fit: cover`, and a warm diagonal **hatch** behind it (`repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, …)`) that shows while the lazy image decodes and stays if it never does — a frame awaiting an image rather than a broken one. No per-row spinner (at 40,000 rows spinners are noise); a **video** whose thumbnail fails to decode overlays a hairline `▷`. **Similarity badge:** 2/8px padding, radius 5, 11px/600, 5px gap, 12px glyph — the per-state border style and weight that carry the meaning in grayscale are untouched. **Decision control:** an **inset track** (`#f3ede3`, 1px hairline, radius 9, 3px padding) 32/28px tall, with segments at radius 7, 26/22px tall, 0 10px / 0 8px padding and a 3px gap; the selected segment is 12px/600 (Delete 700 on its solid red, with no border — the only solid-dark-fill-with-a-light-label in the control), unselected are transparent 12px/500 `ink-muted` with a white-60 % hover wash over the track and no hover on the selected one. Every segment reserves its 1px border as transparent, so staging a decision never shifts the control by a pixel, and the control stays **content-sized** (do not fix a width). **Padlock:** the hit target grows 16 → **28/24px** at radius 6 with a `#f7ebda` hover — the glyph stays 14px, the growth is all hit area, because this is the control that protects a file from bulk operations. **Score cell:** 96/72px, the `keep` label 10px/500 riding above the number (11px mono/600, right-aligned on the same line, 4px under it), then the 6px track; compact drops the label and keeps the number, as does the <940px score collapse. **Cell typography (REPLY L2):** filename 14px at 600 for the keeper and 500 for the rest; the folder sub-line 11px mono `ink-muted` **truncated from the LEFT** (`direction: rtl` + an `ltr` isolate inside) so the leaf — the part that actually distinguishes two copies — stays visible; size / dims 12px mono right-aligned, date 12px mono left; dims lose the spaces around `×` (`4000×3000`), the date loses its numeric form for a spelled month with no time (`14 Mar 2021`, still locale-ordered), and the score number carries **two decimals always** (at one decimal every row in a near-duplicate group printed the same number).
  - **Columns, header chrome and the column budget (layout slice C).** Three changes to the result tree's column model. **(1) The classification column is gone and `Action` heads the decision** (open questions Q1): the classifier enum it printed never varies independently of the similarity badge beside it, and while it sat there the real decision control was the unlabelled thing to its right — the most confusable pair on the row. The decision control now renders **inside** the `action` cell (`data-col="action"`, 168px), the header over it reads **Action** / **動作** (`web.column.action`), and the classification stays reachable as the similarity badge's `title` tooltip in the same `web.classification.*` words. Nothing is renamed: the column id, its `data-col` value, the header/resize testids and `rowDecisionTestid` are unchanged, so the [#735 right-click pre-fill](#web--action-dialog-set-action-by-field) still opens the Set Action dialog on its "Action" field — and that field's seed (`row.user_decision`) now finally matches what the cell shows. **(2) The header is chrome, not the first row of data:** 28px tall *including* its bottom rule, the toolbar surface (`#faf6ee`) with a `#e4d6bd` rule under it, 11px/600 **uppercase** with `.06em` tracking in `ink-muted`. Sorting shows **no glyph when unsorted** (a hairline `▾` on hover) and `▾`/`▴` in the accent with the label promoted to `ink` when sorted; the resize handle is an 8px hit area, invisible at rest, a 2px hairline rule on hover, 2px accent plus a guide line down the body while dragging, and **double-click restores the column's default width**. Column widths from the design's 1280×800 budget (similarity 92, action 168, score 96, dims 88, size 72, date 112, name 160), with **size / dims / score right-aligned** and **size / date / dims / score in `font-mono`** so digits align down the column; the row separator inside a group drops to a lighter `#f4eee3` (the group's closing rule stays `#e4d6bd`). **(3) Need-based column shedding, with File Name as the fill column.** The tree measures its own width (published as `data-table-width`) and gives up a column **only while what is left still does not fit** — automatically, with no user override. Drop order is least-comparable first: **Resolution** (the field most often identical across a duplicate group), then **Shot Date**, then the Score cell collapses to 72px. The design reply quotes 1200 / 1080 / 940px, but those are an *output* of its budget arithmetic with its own widths and chrome, so the arithmetic is what ships and the thresholds are **derived** from the live registry (`requiredWidth` / `shedThresholds`) — the user's own column widths are an input, so a column they narrow can keep a later one on the row and a column they widen can push one off. **File Name fills the leftover width** (`flex: 1 1 <stored>`, `min-width: <stored>`), so what a shed frees lands on the filename rather than in a gutter. At 1280×800 with the preview open the tree measures ~986px: Resolution is shed, **Shot Date stays**, File Name renders 248px against a stored 160px, and 17px of row padding is all that is left to the right of the padlock. Shed columns remain in the preview pane's metadata table, which is always present; neither sortable column (File Name, Size) is sheddable, so a shed can never strand an active sort. **Resizing the fill column:** the drag moves the *stored* width 1:1 (that is what persists and hydrates), while the rendered box is `stored + slack` — so a drag that stays inside the current slack changes the stored width without visibly moving the box, standard fill-column behaviour, and the box never renders narrower than the stored width. Need-based shedding is what keeps that bounded: slack is never more than one about-to-be-shed column wide. Measured — a +120px drag takes the stored width 160 → 280 and the rendered box 250 → 374 (crossing the slack costs Shot Date its place). Under threshold-based shedding the same drag left the box pinned at 374px with 214px of slack, which is why shedding is need-based. **Resizing a SHEDDABLE column (Resolution, Shot Date) can never delete it**, which need-based shedding would otherwise allow — drag its handle past the budget and the column used to unmount under the cursor while the drag kept widening it, then persist a width that hid it with no header cell, no handle and no double-click target left to undo it. Three guards: the shed plan is **frozen for the duration of a drag** and re-planned once on release (so no column disappears mid-gesture, whichever handle is being dragged); a sheddable column's committed width is **capped at the width where it still fits** (measured: a +200px drag on Shot Date at a 986px table commits 202px, not 312px — non-sheddable columns are uncapped, so widening File Name may still cost Shot Date its place); and a stored width that would hide a sheddable column **falls back to that column's default** in both the plan and the store, so a blob written before these guards cannot keep a column hidden.
  - **The group header row, its derived totals and the promoted bulk verb (layout slice G).** The band becomes a real heading: **44px at BOTH densities** (it is the one row that does not compress — the band is the list's only full-width warm fill, so it is what makes a group read as a heading rather than a row), `0 16px 0 12px` padding, a 10px gap, the 16px caret with an 11px glyph, and the title at **14px/700**. The count and a **derived byte total** (`· 18.4 MB`) sit **immediately after the title**, not right-aligned after a spacer — at 1280px a right-aligned count lands in the middle of nowhere once the button takes the right end, and the count is part of the group's identity (open questions Q4, H7). After them comes a **derived folder suffix**: the shared folder's **leaf** when every member sits in one folder, or `· 2 folders` / 「· 2 個資料夾」 when they span more than one — the cross-folder case being the one where deleting the "wrong" copy has consequences for someone's folder structure ([`frontend/src/lib/groupSummary.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/groupSummary.ts), pure functions). Q4 permits a derived total and a derived leaf; it rules out a derived **kind** label ("RAW + JPG pair"), which would infer intent from extensions. The prototype's icon tile (H4) and kind pill (H5) are **dropped by design** — 30×30 of decoration carrying no data, on the one row that should read as a label — so absent is the correct implementation. At the right end sits **"Keep best · delete rest"** / 「保留最佳 · 其餘刪除」, a secondary button (28px, radius 8, `#fffdf9` on a `#ddd2bf` hairline, 12px/600, `#f7f1e7` hover) with **at least 16px between it and the caret's hit area**, because a misclick from collapse into a bulk decision is the expensive misclick on this screen — see [Web — Context menu: Apply best-copy decisions to this group](#web--context-menu-apply-best-copy-decisions-to-this-group) for what it does. The row was a `<button>`; a real `<button>` inside one is invalid HTML, so the row is now `role="button"` with `tabIndex=0` and its own Enter/Space handling — same testid, same `aria-expanded`, same click-anywhere-to-collapse, same place in the tab order — and it carries its own **`aria-label`** ("Group 3, 5 files" / 「群組 3，5 個檔案」, `web.tree.group_aria`), because a `role="button"` with no name of its own is named by its whole subtree and a screen reader would otherwise read the bulk verb as part of the collapse toggle's label. **Dates follow the app's locale, not the browser's:** `formatDate`'s locale argument is optional and every call site passed `undefined`, so an English UI on a zh machine printed 「2024年2月1日」 in the Shot Date column; the row, the preview pane and the metadata table now all take it from `useDateLocale()` (`en → en-GB`, `zh_TW → zh-TW`).
  - **The toolbar, the status bar and the menu-bar scan summary (layout slice TB).** The action strip and the footer get the design reply's geometry, and three controls the app has never had. Both moved out of `App.tsx` into their own components ([`Toolbar.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/Toolbar.tsx), [`StatusBar.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/StatusBar.tsx)); every testid, handler and gate — including the #673 `manifestPath === null` gating on Execute / Set Action and the two additive #712 error lines — moved verbatim. **Toolbar:** 52px, `#faf6ee`, bottom hairline, `0 16px` padding, 8px gap, buttons 34px at radius 8px / 13px. **Exactly one primary** — "Scan", filled `#a85a2c` with a white label, hover `#8f4b23`, active `#7a3f1d`, no shadow and no gradient — «so "where do I start" has exactly one answer»; everything else (Execute, Set Action…, Open, EN, Settings) is secondary on `#fffdf9` with a `#ddd2bf` border. s74/s76 assert the COUNT of warm-filled controls in the strip, not just the colour, so a second accent button fails. The strip is `flex-nowrap` at a fixed height, so the two text inputs are its shrinkable members (`flex 0 1 260px` / `0 1 200px`) and it must FIT rather than wrap — s76 asserts it does not overflow at 1280px, and reports the overflow in px with the per-child widths and the resolved font when it does. **The floors are a measurement, not a guess**: 140/110 came to exactly 1280 under the shipped `Segoe UI` stack with no headroom, and overflowed by 64px on the Linux CI runner, which has no Segoe UI and falls back to a wider face. They are 104px / 56px, which buys 90px back. Below **1100px** of measured strip width the compression runs out, so one group **sheds**: the manifest path input and its Open button ([`lib/toolbarShed.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/toolbarShed.ts), a pure function of the measured width, same shape as slice C's column shedding — and it renders EVERYTHING when the width is unmeasured, so a missing measurement can never silently remove a control). That pair is chosen because it is the only group with an exact duplicate elsewhere: **File → Open Manifest…** does the same job through the filesystem picker, which is the better affordance of the two, so shedding costs a redundant path rather than a capability. It returns when the strip widens. `overflow-x: auto` stays underneath as the last-resort net for a font or width we did not anticipate. **A second stage folds three controls into a "⋯" menu** ([#918](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/918)). With only the manifest pair shed, the strip on a wide (non-Segoe) stack still measured 127px over at 1000px and needs ~1127px, which is more than the 1100 threshold. So at the **same** 1100px width, **Set Action…**, the **language toggle** and **Settings** leave the strip and a secondary **"⋯"** button takes their place at the right, before the divider and the Delete CTA. Its accessible name and tooltip are "More actions" / 「更多動作」. The menu lists **Set Action…** (disabled with no manifest loaded, the same #673 gate as the button), a **Language** group with **English** / **中文 (繁體)** (the active one ticked), and **Settings**. Each item dispatches exactly what the folded button did. Folding language and Settings alone would still have left ~21px of overflow, so Set Action… folds too. Set Action… and the language choice also keep their menu-bar duplicates (Action → Set Action by Field…, View → Language). With all three folded the strip fits down to roughly 890px on that stack. At 1000px s76 now asserts **zero** overflow and that the Delete CTA is fully in view and hit-tests to itself. It also drives ⋯ → Settings and ⋯ → 中文 → English live, and checks that all three buttons come back (and ⋯ goes) at 1280px. **Filter box** (new): 34px, radius 8, leading `⌕`, placeholder "Filter photos…" / 「篩選照片…」, focus border accent plus a 2px inset ring at 35%. It is a **case-insensitive substring match over each row's basename AND folder path** ([`frontend/src/lib/rowFilter.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/rowFilter.ts)) — a review is driven by where a file came from at least as often as by what it is called — applied once in `ResultTree` so the sort, the flatten, the keyboard range and the group header's own count and size total all describe what is on screen; **a group with no matching row is hidden entirely**, because an empty header promises rows that are not there. It is **view-only**: it never writes a decision, a lock or the manifest, it is deliberately NOT persisted (a filter that came back after a reload would hide rows with no visible cause), and clearing it restores everything exactly. It also never reaches the delete counter or the danger CTA — those count the whole manifest, which is what Execute acts on. **Counted bulk verbs** (open questions L5): `Set 3 selected: Keep · Delete · Skip` / 「設定 3 個選取項目：保留 · 刪除 · 略過」, reading `Set selected:` and disabled at zero. The count is IN the label «so "Delete" never means an unknown number». They act on the **visible** part of the existing ctrl/shift row selection: the selection survives the filter (typing must not destroy the user's picks, and clearing gives them back), so it can name rows nobody can see — `visibleSelectedPaths` ([`lib/rowFilter.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/rowFilter.ts)) is the ONE selector the label and the write both read, so the number shown and the number written cannot drift. They act on the ctrl/shift row selection — **no per-row checkbox** (at 1280px it costs 18px from the column already down to 81px, and duplicates a selection model that works) and **no select-all** («at 40,000 rows "all" is a trap»); a tooltip carries the Ctrl / Shift hint. They **respect locks silently-but-visibly**, the same promise Q5 gave the keep-best button: locked rows are filtered out client-side rather than sent and refused, because the 409 path raises the LockConfirmDialog that Q5 ruled out in front of a reversible bulk decision, and the undo toast reports "N locked file(s) unchanged" instead. **Danger CTA:** far right, after a flex spacer plus a `1px × 22px` hairline divider, 34px at 13px/700 on `danger-warm` with a white label, opening the same Execute dialog the toolbar's Execute button does. Its label carries the live count — `⊗ Delete 4 files…` / 「⊗ 刪除 4 個檔案…」 — «the difference between a button that deletes something and a button that deletes *four things*, and it is read right before the confirm modal». At zero it is **disabled but still present and still counting** ("Delete 0 files…") «so the button never moves»; a destructive control that appears under the cursor is one that gets pressed by a click aimed at something else. Its hover darkens `danger-warm` with a filter rather than adding a fifth red to the palette. **Status bar:** the 30px height and the 11.5px type sit on the STRIP (`main-status-strip`), not on the `<footer>` — the two conditional error lines are siblings below it, so a fixed-height footer would clip them, and `[data-testid=main-status-bar].closest('footer')` still resolves for the scenarios that read the surface through it. Left to right: the existing `N groups · M files` summary, then #906's two figures — `⊗ N marked to delete` at 600 in `danger-warm` and `↺ reclaims X MB` through the same `formatBytes` every other size uses — then a spacer, the `Density` label and the segmented control. Both figures render from a manifest load onward, at zero as well, for the same reason the CTA does: a counter that exists only when non-zero cannot answer "did that write land". **Menu bar (F3/F4):** the prototype's second titlebar is **not drawn** — «two stacked chrome bars cost 73px of an 800px viewport and give the user nothing the other does not» — and its one piece of content is folded into the 33px menu bar instead, right-aligned at 11.5px: `5 files · 1 group · near-duplicates` (`main-scan-summary`), never wrapping, with the **source name as the only shrinkable part** so it is what truncates. The source name has no field of its own: E7 (recent sources) was dropped by design including its scan-history store, so it is DERIVED from data already loaded — the deepest folder every row shares, falling back to the manifest file's own name when the rows span unrelated roots ([`frontend/src/lib/scanSummary.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/scanSummary.ts)). Nothing to invalidate, and no stale path on the app's first screen.
  - **The empty state, the similarity legend and the caution banners (layout slice E).** Three surfaces, one slice. **(1) The empty state** ([`App.tsx`](../frontend/src/App.tsx)) was one 14px muted sentence ("No manifest loaded — Scan or Open a manifest to begin.") between two identical 32px neutral buttons. It becomes a centred 520px column: one 96px tile, a **25px/700 heading** ("Find duplicate & similar photos"／「找出重複與相似的照片」), body copy at 14px/1.6 capped at 430px that says what the app *does*, and the two CTAs at **h48 / r12** of which exactly one is filled with the accent — the same single-primary rule the toolbar follows. Both handlers and both testids (`main-empty-scan` → Scan dialog, `main-empty-open` → manifest picker) are unchanged. Two things the design prototype draws are deliberately **not built**: the "Nothing is deleted until you review and confirm" safety pill (open questions Q10, dropped — no safety pill is drawn anywhere in the app) and the **Recent sources** list (audit E7, dropped by design — it needs a scan-history store whose stale paths would fail on the app's very first screen; the cheap version, if it ever returns, is the last-scanned path echoed in the Open control's placeholder). **(2) The similarity legend** ([`SimilarityLegend.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/SimilarityLegend.tsx)) is pinned to the bottom of the preview pane, outside its scroller, and is visible whether or not a file is selected: five rows, each a real badge (the same `lib/similarityBadge` spec and `similarityLabel()` the row cell uses, at `min-width: 74px` so the pills line up) beside a phrase for what the state means — ★ Ref «chosen keeper», 100% «exact duplicate», 96% «near match», ★ 95% «linked indirectly», — «video / no image». The meanings previously existed only in the cell's hover tooltip. **(3) The caution banners** ([`cautionBanner.ts`](../frontend/src/components/execute/cautionBanner.ts)) — the execute dialog's all-delete warning and its hidden-destructive line take the Q9 role described above, and the ⚠ that led the hidden-destructive sentence leaves the **copy** in both catalogs because the component now renders the ▲. The post-execute split is kept deliberately: "Files Not Found" is caution (the outcome the user asked for, reported), "Files Failed to Delete" stays danger.
- **Density preference (layout slice R):** `resultView.density` — `"comfortable"` (default) or `"compact"` — persisted per browser under its own `density` localStorage key ([`frontend/src/lib/density.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/density.ts)), hydrated once at store creation and merged over the default, exactly like the #739 panel widths and the #685 column widths. A value that is not one of the two falls back to comfortable rather than rendering rows with no height. **The toggle UI is layout slice TB** — the segmented Comfortable/Compact control at the right end of the status strip (`main-density-toggle`, with `main-density-comfortable` / `main-density-compact` segments), right-aligned «because a view preference belongs furthest from the data». The compact density also raises the same "compact score cell" flag the <940px collapse raises, so the header cell and the body cells stay one width. **A density change forces the virtualiser to re-measure** ([`ResultTree.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/ResultTree.tsx)): `@tanstack/react-virtual` caches a size per index and re-reads `estimateSize` only when told to, so without an explicit `measure()` the rows shrink to 52px while the total scroll height and every offset past the first screen stay 72px-based — visible as a keyboard scroll landing short, never as a failing unit test (`overscan: 10` buffers it near the top). The call is gated on an actual CHANGE, not merely on `density` being an effect dependency: `measure()` DISCARDS the cache, so an unconditional call throws away the sizes the first layout pass just measured.
- **Conditions / variants:** The shadcn token block (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--primary`, …) is remapped onto the same palette so every `ui/*` primitive inherits it without a per-component edit. The `.dark` block points the Daylight surface / text / border tokens at the existing dark neutrals, so dark mode keeps the appearance it has today; the dark "Aperture" palette is out of scope for this slice, and the badge / decision chips and the score fill are deliberately shared between the two (the score *track*, being a surface, is remapped like the rest). A **locked** row's decision control stays disabled, as it was before the retone. The header padlock's horizontal alignment over the lock column is decorative and tuned to the English decision labels; a wider translation slides the glyph a few px and changes nothing else. **Media chrome stays dark on purpose** (slice e): the full-resolution viewer's title/footer bars and buttons, the preview pane's image viewport, the grid's video transport bar, and the thumbnail caption scrims are the surfaces a photo or video is shown *on*, so they keep the neutral dark they had — DAYLIGHT defines no dark-chrome role, and the dark "Aperture" palette is out of scope for #878. The media-chrome allowlist is now the whole of it: **the amber group is gone.** Slice e left the execute dialog's warning banners on Tailwind's stock `amber-*` scale as the one off-palette exception, and **layout slice E retired it** — both banners and the post-execute "Files Not Found" list wear the Q9 caution role (`#fdf3d9` fill, `#6b4e12` ink, `#e3c264` frame with a 3px left rule, ▲ glyph), so no `amber-*` or `yellow-*` utility remains anywhere under `frontend/src`.
- **Related:** [#878](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/878) slices (a) + (b) + (e) — theme foundation + tree visuals, then decision chips / padlock / score bar, then the sweep that finished the foundation outside the tree; the design and its verbatim tokens come from the closed Qt PR [#639](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/pull/639) and `docs/audits/web-port-feasibility-2026-06-19.md` §9.3. Layout slice C — the column model, the header chrome and the 1280×800 column budget — comes from the design's `REPLY - review screen layout` (L1 flat-table spec, L2 mono cells, L3 column budget) and `REPLY - review screen open questions` Q1. Layout slice R — the row box, the thumbnail, the decision track, the padlock, the score cell, the cell typography and the two density value sets — comes from the same reply's L1 (last-row breath), L2 (cell typography) and L3 (§Row heights / Thumbnail / Similarity badge / Decision control / Padlock / Score bar). Layout slice G — the group header's geometry, its derived byte total and folder suffix, and the promoted "Keep best · delete rest" button with its undo toast — comes from the same reply's §"Group header (H1, H4–H7)" and open questions Q4 (what a group header may derive about itself) + Q5 (promote the verb, no confirm, undo instead, never override a lock). Layout slice TB — the 52px toolbar with its single primary, the filter box, the counted bulk verbs and the Delete-N CTA, plus the 30px status strip with #906's two figures and the density switch, and the scan summary folded into the menu bar — comes from the same reply's §"Toolbar (F5, F6, F8, F9)" and §"Status bar (F11, F12)" and open questions L5 (no row checkbox, counted verbs against the ctrl/shift selection, menu bar only with the summary right-aligned, recent sources dropped). It **closes [#906](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/906)** (delete counter + reclaim total); the Execute-confirm counterpart of that issue (the delete total, folder subtotals and per-file reason lines inside the confirm) is tracked as [#917](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/917) and ships as its own PR after the slice programme. Layout slice E — the empty state, the similarity legend footer and the caution banners — comes from the same reply's L4 verdicts (E1/E3/E4/E5 MUST-MATCH, E2 simplified to one tile, E7 dropped), its L5 «Recent sources — drop the section», audit row P8 (legend), and open questions Q9 (the caution banner and chip spec) with Q10 dropped. **With slice E the layout programme's #878 slices (c) density toggle + toolbar and (d) legend footer + empty-state CTA are complete** — the seven slices shipped in order T → C → R → G → TB → PV → E; no layout slice remains open (#917 is the one follow-up outside the programme). Badge rendering detail under [Similarity column](#similarity-column). Decision semantics under [Web — manifest decision persistence](#web--manifest-decision-persistence). QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py) (the legend's pinned position and the caution banner's computed colours ride here) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s78_empty_state.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s78_empty_state.py) (the empty state in both locales); unit tests [`frontend/src/App.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/App.test.tsx), [`frontend/src/components/SimilarityLegend.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/SimilarityLegend.test.tsx) and [`frontend/src/components/execute/banners.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/execute/banners.test.tsx).

### Similarity column

- **Entry point:** The **Similarity** column of the main result tree, right of File Name (header key `web.column.similarity`; the column order lives in [frontend/src/lib/resultColumns.ts](../frontend/src/lib/resultColumns.ts)), rendered per file row by [frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.tsx](../frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.tsx) from the `similarity` object that `GET /api/manifest` serializes for every row ([core/app_service/review_view.py](../core/app_service/review_view.py)). The preview pane repeats the same badge for the selected file, and a legend pinned to the bottom of the pane ([frontend/src/components/SimilarityLegend.tsx](../frontend/src/components/SimilarityLegend.tsx)) names the five states.
- **Trigger:** Populated automatically when a manifest loads; no user action.
- **Behaviour:** The server picks at most one **Ref** per group — the highest-score row among the Ref-tier rows (classification `KEEP`, `UNDATED` or none), an unscored row losing to a scored one, with the lexicographic file path as the final tie-break ([#241](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/241)); a group with no Ref-tier row (e.g. all `EXACT`) has no Ref. Each file row then gets one `similarity.kind`, which [frontend/src/lib/format.ts](../frontend/src/lib/format.ts) turns into the cell text:
  - `ref` → **Ref** (`web.format.similarity_ref`) — the Ref winner.
  - `percent` 100 → **100%** — classification `EXACT` (SHA / format duplicate).
  - `percent` N → **N%** — `REVIEW_DUPLICATE`, computed server-side as `round((64 - hamming) / 64 * 100)`, where `hamming` is the pHash Hamming distance between the row and **the displayed Ref**, not the scanner's anchor ([#253](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/253)).
  - `passenger` → **★ N%** — a Ref-tier row that did not win the Ref pick (e.g. a same-stem companion, a [#824](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/824) complementary RAW + lossy pair, or a [#538](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/538)-reconnected member), measured against the displayed Ref the same way; the ★ marks an indirect / transitive member ([#536](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/536) Direction A).
  - `near_dup` → **~dup** (`web.format.similarity_near_dup`; zh_TW `~近似`) — a `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` row with no computable distance.
  - `none` → **—** — a Ref-tier passenger with no comparable pHash (e.g. a Live Photo MOV).

  The cell is a pill whose colour and two grayscale-safe cues — border style and font weight — differ per state ([#878](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/878) slice (a), [frontend/src/lib/similarityBadge.ts](../frontend/src/lib/similarityBadge.ts)): `ref` → gold, solid, semibold, with a leading `aria-hidden` ★ outside the label span (so the queryable cell text stays exactly the label); `percent` at 100 → purple, solid, bold; `percent` under 100 **and** `near_dup` → blue, solid, medium (one relationship, told apart by their labels); `passenger` → gray, **dashed**, medium; `none` → muted, **dotted**, normal. The badge keys on the percentage, so a `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` row at distance 0 from the Ref wears the 100 % pill too. Hovering the badge shows the row's classification in words (Exact copy / Near-duplicate / Best in group / No date). The keeper row carries no second "Ref" pill (copy audit R7 / design Q2, 2026-09-18): besides this badge it is readable from the left of the row by its filename at weight 600 (500 elsewhere) and a 2 px accent strip on its left edge (`border-l-warm`); every row reserves those 2 px, so the strip costs no horizontal shift.
- **Conditions / variants:** The render-time recomputation needs both the displayed Ref's pHash and the row's pHash. When either is missing (old manifests without the pHash column, video rows, or `imagehash` not installed — [scanner/phash_distance.py](../scanner/phash_distance.py) then returns `None`), a `REVIEW_DUPLICATE` row falls back to the scanner's stored `hamming_distance` (relative to the scanner's anchor, not the displayed Ref), and only with neither does it read `~dup`. The scanner still writes `hamming_distance`, but it is not the source of truth for the rendered percentage whenever pHashes are available. The column is not sortable (only File Name and Size are).
- **Related:** [#253](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/253) (render against the displayed Ref); [#241](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/241) (score-aware Ref tie-break); [#536](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/536) (Direction A — the starred passenger); [#824](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/824); [#878](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/878); helper [scanner/phash_distance.py](../scanner/phash_distance.py); unit tests [`tests/test_review_view.py`](../tests/test_review_view.py) (Ref pick and every similarity kind), [`frontend/src/lib/format.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/format.test.ts) (labels, including `~dup`), [`frontend/src/lib/similarityBadge.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/similarityBadge.test.ts) (the five `(glyph, border, weight)` triples stay distinct), [`frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/result/FileRow.test.tsx) ("Ref" appears exactly once on a keeper row, inside the badge); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s52_similarity_against_displayed_ref.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s52_similarity_against_displayed_ref.py) (percent against the displayed Ref via `GET /api/manifest`; exactly one Ref), [`qa/web/scenarios/s65_passenger_bridge.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s65_passenger_bridge.py) (a passenger-bridge group forms with a pHash on every member) and [`qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s74_daylight_theme.py) (computed border-style and font-weight off the live badges — jsdom sees only class names). Visual framing under [Web — Daylight theme and results-tree framing](#web--daylight-theme-and-results-tree-framing).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client); 2026-09-14 (copy audit R6 — near-dup label `~dup` restored and translated); 2026-09-13 (#878 slice (a) — web 5-state badge, live-run s74); 2026-09-04 (#824 — complementary RAW+lossy pairs join the passenger class)

### Preview pane — byte-budget LRU cache

- **Entry point:** `infrastructure/image_service.py`: `ImageService.__init__` and `_ByteBudgetLRUCache`. The web server creates one `ImageService` at start-up (`app/web/main.py` lifespan) and `GET /api/image` ([app/web/routes/image.py](../app/web/routes/image.py)) uses it.
- **Trigger:** Every `GET /api/image` the web client makes. Result-tree row thumbnails, grid tiles, the preview-pane image and Execute-dialog thumbnails ask for `size=512`. The full-resolution viewer asks for `size=0`.
- **Behaviour:**
  - The in-memory image cache evicts by byte budget instead of item count. There are two tiers: a thumbnail tier for requests whose longest side is ≤ 256 px, and a preview tier for everything larger, including full-res (`size=0`).
  - Total budget = `min(256 MB, RAM // 32)`, split 1:3 between the tiers (≈64 MB / ≈192 MB with ≥ 8 GB RAM; 16 MB floor each). When a tier exceeds its budget, the least-recently-used entry is evicted.
  - The web client asks only for 512 px or full size, so everything it loads lands in the preview tier. The thumbnail tier serves only requests that omit `size` (the route default is 256).
  - **Disk cache:** versioned files under `~/AppData/Local/PhotoManager/thumbs/v1/<sha1>.jpg` by default. The settings key `thumbnail_disk_cache_dir` moves that root (environment variables are expanded), and its sibling `video_transcode_cache_dir` moves the video-transcode cache the same way. The web server's lifespan loads `settings.json` through `load_settings()` and hands it to both `ImageService` and `TranscodeService` ([#874](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/874)). Before #874, the web client constructed both services with no settings and wrote every cache to `%LOCALAPPDATA%` no matter what was configured.
  - The **byte budget itself is not a settings key**; it comes from the RAM probe above. A recipe-version bump invalidates the old namespace.
  - On the first start after an upgrade, unversioned `.jpg` files directly under `thumbs/` are deleted. The web server builds `ImageService` without a status reporter, so the one-time "Rebuilding thumbnail cache (version N) — this is a one-time operation." message reaches the server log only.
- **Cache-key freshness (web audit Correction #11):** the cache key is `sha1(path|size|source_mtime_ns)`. The source mtime is folded in so that overwriting a photo in place (a crop or re-export saved to the same path by another program) produces a fresh key, instead of the disk/memory cache serving the pre-edit bytes indefinitely. `GET /api/image` stats the source once per request and threads that value into both the decode call and the disk-cache-path lookup used for the ETag, so an in-place edit also changes the ETag without a second stat. Responses carry `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400, stale-while-revalidate=604800` (full-res: `max-age=3600`), so within that window the browser may keep showing its own cached copy until it revalidates.
- **Conditions / variants:**
  - **DNG fast path:** DNG files use the embedded JPEG (rawpy `extract_thumb`) when the embedded image's longest side is ≥ the requested side. That means ≥ 512 px for the web's previews and thumbnails; any embedded JPEG qualifies for a full-res request. A full `postprocess` decode runs only when the embedded image is too small or absent.
  - **EXIF Orientation is applied** to the embedded JPEG with Pillow's `ImageOps.exif_transpose` before it is re-encoded, so the JPEG the server returns is already upright. Without this, portrait-grip iPhone ProRAW DNGs (which store landscape pixels + Orientation=6/8 in the embedded JPEG's EXIF) would be served 90° rotated relative to Lightroom / File Explorer. The bitmap (non-JPEG) thumb branch is unaffected: rawpy delivers that array in the correct orientation natively.
  - **#865 — the embedded JPEG is decoded in draft mode.** For a capped request, the JPEG branch calls `Image.draft` before `exif_transpose`, so libjpeg decodes at the largest 1/8, 1/4 or 1/2 DCT reduction that meets the cap instead of at full size, and the transpose and LANCZOS shrink run on that smaller image. `draft` must precede `exif_transpose`: `exif_transpose` calls `load()`, after which `draft` is a silent no-op (the same idiom and ordering as the hasher's #569/#822 draft). Full-res requests (`size=0`) never draft. The "too small → fall through" test is judged from the pre-draft header size and runs before any pixel is decoded, so a rejected thumb no longer costs a full decode and a transposed copy first.
  - **The one visible trade-off — an undershoot of up to 2 % of the cap:** the DCT scaler offers only the 1/2, 1/4 and 1/8 steps, so a frame whose next step lands just under the cap would otherwise decode one step larger than needed (a 4032 px frame at the 512 cap would take 1/4 instead of 1/8). `_DRAFT_UNDERSHOOT = 0.02` accepts a long edge up to 2 % under the cap when that buys a whole step; a frame whose next step falls further under the cap decodes at the next larger step — at full size only when even the 1/2 step would undershoot — and is then shrunk exactly onto the cap. The draft box is matched to the image's aspect ratio, not square: `draft` constrains **both** axes (`min(w // req_w, h // req_h)`) while `thumbnail` fits only the long edge, so a square request would make the short edge binding and decline reductions that are fine.
  - **Where the #865 numbers come from:** they were measured on the J: NAS through the retired desktop preview stack at its 2048 px cap. There, iPhone ProRAW's embedded frame is the whole 8064×6048 sensor ([#826](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/826)), so a click decoded 3 MP rather than 48 MP. This library's 4032×3024 frames sit 1.6 % above the half-scale step for a 2048 cap, so they rendered at 2016 px instead of 2048 (≤ 41 px at that cap). The rule is relative to the cap: at the web's 512 px request the tolerance is ≤ 10 px, and the same 4032×3024 frames take the 1/8 step and come back at 504 px.
- **Related:** [#622](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/622) Phase 1 + Phase 3 measurement; [#865](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/865); [#874](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/874); `infrastructure/image_service.py`; `tests/test_image_service.py`; `app/web/routes/image.py`; `app/web/main.py`; `tests/test_web_image_route.py`; `docs/audits/preview-phase3-results-2026-09.md`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client: request sizes, tier routing and the log-only cache-wipe notice read from code); 2026-07-16 (web audit remediation — source-mtime cache-key freshness, Correction #11); 2026-09-07 (#865 draft-mode embedded decode, re-measured on the J: NAS through the retired desktop preview stack per `docs/audits/preview-phase3-results-2026-09.md`); 2026-09-07 (#874 — the web lifespan passes settings to both media services; verified against a live server on port 8782 with both cache dirs pointed at temp directories: the thumbnail and the H.264 transcode landed there, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%/PhotoManager/{thumbs,transcodes}` gained 0 files)

### Preview pane — per-device request coordinator + 1-ahead prefetch

- **Entry point:** None — removed with the desktop client in #646. [#887](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/887) tracks measuring whether the web client needs one, and porting it if the NAS numbers say so.
- **Trigger:** The desktop coordinator ran on every single-view preview and grid-thumbnail request.
- **Behaviour:**
  - The desktop client serialised image decodes per physical device, using the `infrastructure/device_key.py` bucket the scanner uses ([#565](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/565)). It dropped superseded clicks before they read from disk ("D waits for A"), discarded a finished image whose selection had moved on, and prefetched the next group's first image.
  - **The web client has none of this.** Each `<img>` fetches `GET /api/image` on its own. FastAPI runs that plain-`def` handler in its worker threadpool ([#790](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/790)) with no per-device queue and no cancellation check, so a decode the server has started runs to completion even after the browser has moved on.
  - Result-tree, grid-tile and Execute-dialog thumbnails use the browser's native `loading="lazy"`. There is no prefetch.
  - The server-side decode throttles in `infrastructure/image_service.py` are `_FULLRES_DECODE_SEM = threading.BoundedSemaphore(2)`, around full-resolution `rawpy` demosaics, and the Shell/WIC thumbnail path's two-worker `_wic_executor` (each call waits at most 10 s). Neither is per device.
- **Conditions / variants:** **Source-mtime stat TTL:** `_MtimeStatCache` (5 s TTL, bounded to 512 paths) still exists in `infrastructure/image_service.py`, but only callers that do not pass a source mtime use it. `GET /api/image` always stats the source itself and passes that value in, so no web request goes through the TTL. An in-place edit is picked up on the next request, not after up to 5 s.
- **Related:** [#622](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/622) Phase 2 (the desktop coordinator; its Phase 3 measurement is in `docs/audits/preview-phase3-results-2026-09.md`); [#887](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/887); `infrastructure/image_service.py` (`_FULLRES_DECODE_SEM`, `_MtimeStatCache`); `app/web/routes/image.py`; unit tests `tests/test_image_service.py` (`TestMtimeStatCacheTTL`); `infrastructure/device_key.py` is now used only by the scanner.
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten as removed history; `git grep -i "device_key\|coordinator\|prefetch" -- app/web frontend/src infrastructure/image_service.py` finds nothing)

### Preview pane — full-resolution viewer

- **Entry point:** [frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx](../frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx). A single overlay is mounted in `App.tsx` and is open while `store.preview.fullResPath` is set (`store.openFullRes(path)` / `closeFullRes()`). The image comes from `GET /api/image?path=…&size=0` (`fullResUrl` in `frontend/src/api/client.ts`).
- **Trigger:** Double-click on the preview pane's image (`preview-single-image`), on an image tile in the group grid, or on a file row in the result tree. Double-clicking a video in the preview pane or a video grid tile does nothing; double-clicking a video file ROW opens the overlay with a video player.
- **Behaviour:**
  - The viewer is a modal overlay that fills the viewport by default and can be moved and resized ([Web — overlay window geometry (movable / resizable / persisted)](#web--overlay-window-geometry-movable--resizable--persisted)).
  - A spinner shows until the image loads. The title bar shows the file name and, once loaded, the served image's pixel size (`W × H`).
  - **Zoom:** Ctrl + mouse wheel, ±10 % per wheel event, clamped to 5 %–800 % ([#743](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/743)). **Pan:** drag with the left button. **Close:** Esc, the ✕ in the title bar, or a click on the empty area around the image. A footer hint reads "Ctrl+scroll to zoom · Drag to pan · Esc to close".
  - **Server side:** the file is decoded at its own size and returned as a JPEG. A DNG serves its embedded preview image (EXIF-rotated) whenever it has one, at whatever size it was stored. For iPhone ProRAW that is the full sensor, but a DNG with a small embedded preview shows that small image. Only a DNG without an embedded image goes through a full `rawpy` demosaic, at most two at a time (`_FULLRES_DECODE_SEM`).
  - The full-res JPEG is cached like any other request, in the preview tier of the byte-budget LRU and on disk ([Preview pane — byte-budget LRU cache](#preview-pane--byte-budget-lru-cache)), and is sent with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`.
- **Conditions / variants:**
  - **One viewer at a time.** Opening another file replaces the one shown. The retired desktop client opened a separate, non-modal window per double-click.
  - **Size limit:** source files larger than **40 MiB** get HTTP 413 before any decode. The viewer cannot read the status code, so any image load failure shows "File too large for in-browser full-res preview." with an "Open in default app" button. Despite its label, that button reveals the file in Windows Explorer (`POST /api/reveal` → `explorer /select,`).
  - **Video rows:** the overlay shows a native `<video controls>` (no autoplay, no zoom/pan) with the same H.264 transcode fallback as the preview pane ([Web preview pane — video transcode fallback (V2 H.264)](#web-preview-pane--video-transcode-fallback-v2-h264)).
- **Related:** [#622](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/622) Phase 1 (PR #624); [#743](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/743) (zoom clamp); [#739](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/739) (movable / resizable); `frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx`; `frontend/src/api/client.ts` (`fullResUrl`); `app/web/routes/image.py` (`size=0`, 413 guard); `infrastructure/image_service.py` (`_FULLRES_DECODE_SEM`); unit tests `tests/test_web_image_route.py` (full-res 200 + Cache-Control, 413 for a large source); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s68_full_res_viewer_double_click.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s68_full_res_viewer_double_click.py) (double-click the preview image → viewer shows the basename and `W × H` → Esc closes it), [`qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s39_layout_persist.py) (viewer geometry).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Preview pane — no-autoplay video default

- **Entry point:** [frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.tsx](../frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.tsx) (single-file `<video>`), [frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx](../frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx) (viewer `<video>`), [frontend/src/components/VideoTile.tsx](../frontend/src/components/VideoTile.tsx) (grid tiles).
- **Trigger:** Selecting a video file row (single view), double-clicking a video file row (viewer), or selecting a group row that contains videos (grid view).
- **Behaviour:** Nothing plays on selection.
  - The single-view pane and the full-res viewer render a native `<video controls>` with no `autoplay`, so playback waits for the user's Play.
  - In the grid, each video tile is a static poster frame with a ▶ glyph and no `<video>` element until it is clicked. The click creates a `<video controls autoPlay>`, so that one tile starts playing at once.
  - The group controller bar ([Web preview pane — group sync controller (GroupMediaController)](#web-preview-pane--group-sync-controller-groupmediacontroller)) broadcasts only to tiles that have been clicked. Its one-shot `playAll()` fires only after every video tile in the group has been clicked.
- **Conditions / variants:** The native controls' Play starts playback as usual. When the browser cannot decode a source, all three surfaces use the same transcode fallback ([Web preview pane — video transcode fallback (V2 H.264)](#web-preview-pane--video-transcode-fallback-v2-h264)). Selecting another group unmounts the whole grid, so no tile keeps playing in the background.
- **Related:** [#622](https://github.com/jackal998/photo-manager/issues/622) Phase 1 (PR #624); `frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.tsx`; `frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx`; `frontend/src/components/VideoTile.tsx`; component tests [`frontend/src/components/VideoTile.test.tsx`](../frontend/src/components/VideoTile.test.tsx) (no `<video>` before the click; the click-mounted `<video>` carries `autoplay`); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s69_video_playback.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s69_video_playback.py) (single-view `<video>` decodes and plays when told to), [`qa/web/scenarios/s71_grid_video_tiles.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s71_grid_video_tiles.py) (grid click-to-play, then group pause / play).
- **Last verified:** 2026-09-30 (#888 — rewritten against the shipped web client)

### Web preview pane — video transcode fallback (V2 H.264)

- **Entry point:** `infrastructure/transcode_service.py` (`TranscodeService`, `_resolve_media_tool`, `_select_h264_encoder`); `.github/workflows/release.yml` + `pyinstaller.spec` (the bundled ffmpeg, #854); `app/web/routes/media.py` (`GET /api/media?transcode=h264`); `frontend/src/components/PreviewPane.tsx`; `frontend/src/components/FullResViewer.tsx`; `frontend/src/components/VideoTile.tsx`; `frontend/src/lib/videoCapabilities.ts` (#787 capability pre-check).
- **Trigger:** The browser's `<video>` element fires an `error` event when the native codec cannot decode the source (e.g. HEVC on a browser without the OS H.265 codec pack). Since #787 a second, earlier trigger exists: the transcode `src` is chosen **up front**, with no failed attempt at all, when the capability pre-check has already established that this engine cannot decode HEVC.
- **Behaviour:** On first decode error the frontend swaps the `<video>` `src` from `/api/media?path=…` to `/api/media?path=…&transcode=h264` ONCE (swap-once, never loops). The React `key` includes the transcode flag so the element remounts and re-attempts decode on swap. While the transcode is being prepared, a "Preparing video…" indicator is shown (both PreviewPane and FullResViewer since #737). On a second error (transcode also failed) a terminal "Video cannot be played" message is displayed. The backend lazily transcodes the source to H.264 MP4 using ffmpeg (`libx264 -preset ultrafast -crf 23`, `aac` audio, `-movflags +faststart`) and caches the result under `AppData/Local/PhotoManager/transcodes/v1/<sha1>.mp4`. **Codec passthrough (#853):** on a cache miss the service first asks `ffprobe` once what the source actually holds (`-print_format json -show_entries stream=codec_type,codec_name,pix_fmt`, argv list, 30 s timeout); when the first video stream is already `h264` **in a browser-decodable pixel format** (`yuv420p` or `yuvj420p` — 8-bit 4:2:0) and the FIRST audio stream (if any) is `aac`/`mp3`, the cached MP4 is produced by a stream COPY (`-map 0:v:0 -map 0:a:0? -c copy -movflags +faststart -f mp4`) instead of a re-encode — the video bitstream is copied bit-for-bit, so there is no quality loss and no encode wait. Anything else — HEVC, VP9, AV1, or an audio track the browser cannot decode (PCM, AC-3, Opus) — keeps the `libx264` encode unchanged. **The pixel format is part of the verdict** because `codec_name == "h264"` also covers High 10 / High 4:2:2 / High 4:4:4 (10-bit and 4:2:2 output from Sony and Panasonic bodies and some screen recorders), which no mainstream browser decodes; copying one through would produce a file the `<video>` element refuses. For the same reason the encode now forces `-pix_fmt yuv420p` instead of inheriting the source's — without it libx264 re-encodes a 10-bit source back to 10-bit, so routing it to the encode would have achieved nothing. A missing `pix_fmt` in the probe output counts as "no verdict" and takes the encode. **Cache caveat:** `TRANSCODE_RECIPE_VERSION` was deliberately not bumped for this, so a cache entry created before this change from a non-4:2:0 source keeps its old (still 10-bit) bytes until the source's `mtime` changes or the cache directory is cleared. **Only the first audio stream is judged (#862)** because it is the only one the copy can produce — `-map 0:a:0?` drops every later track — so a camera/screen-recorder file with AAC first and a PCM commentary track second is now remuxed instead of taking a full encode whose output was the same `h264 + aac` pair. A non-copyable FIRST audio track (PCM first, AAC second) still takes the encode. Cache key = `sha1(source_path | mtime_ns)` so the cache auto-invalidates when the file changes. Concurrent requests for the same source serialise on a per-key lock; total concurrent **media sub-processes are bounded to 2 by one `BoundedSemaphore` — the ffprobe verdict shares the cap with ffmpeg since #862**, so a burst of cold requests for distinct files can no longer hold one 30 s-timeout ffprobe each against the same disk (measured before the fix: 8 simultaneous requests → 8 concurrent ffprobe). The source path is validated against allowed roots (path guard) before transcoding; the service-internal cache path is never exposed to the guard. **Where ffmpeg comes from (#854):** the packaged release BUNDLES its own `ffmpeg.exe` + `ffprobe.exe` — `.github/workflows/release.yml` downloads one pinned, SHA-256-verified BtbN win64 **LGPL shared** build (tag `autobuild-2026-09-05-13-10`, asset `ffmpeg-n9.0.1-26-g5c8e7e2433-win64-lgpl-shared-9.0.zip`) and `pyinstaller.spec` ships the two exes, their seven `av*`/`sw*` DLLs and the upstream licence in `_internal\ffmpeg\`. The *shared* variant is deliberate: the static one costs 218 MiB unpacked against 127.9 MiB for the same binaries sharing one set of libraries (measured). The **sub-directory** is deliberate too — the exes resolve their DLLs from their own directory first, and keeping ours in their own `_internal\ffmpeg\` folder is what stopped them resolving against the second FFmpeg DLL set the pre-#646 bundle also carried. `TranscodeService` resolves each tool in a fixed order: `ffmpeg\` next to `photo-manager.exe`, then `_internal\ffmpeg\` (`sys._MEIPASS`), then the same two locations loose, then PATH, then `None` — so a user can override the shipped build by dropping a complete set in an `ffmpeg` folder beside the exe, and a dev checkout still uses PATH exactly as before. Only when all of that finds nothing does the route return `HTTP 501`. Because a *LGPL* build is deliberately compiled `--disable-libx264` (x264 is GPL), the encoder is no longer hard-coded: the service asks the resolved binary once (`ffmpeg -encoders`, at init) and uses `libx264` when present (dev machines, CI, chocolatey/apt builds — the historical `-preset ultrafast -crf 23` recipe unchanged) and Cisco's `libopenh264` otherwise (`-b:v 4M`, the bundled build's software H.264 encoder); a probe that cannot run falls back to the historical recipe. Both recipes still force `-pix_fmt yuv420p` and `aac` audio, so the output is the same browser-playable shape either way. The encode is fully synchronous — the first byte waits for the whole file — so the `ultrafast` preset (#737) minimises the first-view stall on the owner's ~99%-HEVC library (output size is irrelevant for a throwaway cached stream); repeat views are near-instant cache hits. A passthrough remux is synchronous too but is I/O-bound rather than CPU-bound: measured on a 10 s 720p clip through the real route on a cold cache, first-byte was 0.14 s for the stream copy against 0.47 s for the encode of the byte-identical video stream (`curl -w '%{time_starttransfer}'` against `GET /api/media?...&transcode=h264`; both clips carried the same testsrc video stream, one with AAC audio and one with PCM to force the encode — see #853). Progressive streaming + a real progress % is a tracked follow-up.
- **Conditions / variants:** The fallback only fires on error — native-codec sources play without transcoding overhead. The `mediaUrl(path, { transcode: "h264" })` helper in `client.ts` appends `&transcode=h264`; zero-arg callers are unaffected (backward-compatible). **Capability pre-check (#787):** `frontend/src/lib/videoCapabilities.ts` asks `navigator.mediaCapabilities.decodingInfo` once per page life (module-level memo, shared by all three video surfaces) whether the engine can decode HEVC — `video/mp4; codecs="hvc1.1.6.L93.B0"`, then `hev1…`, at 1080p30. The result is three-state: a real "no" from the API is `unsupported`; a missing API (jsdom, pre-2019 engines) or a throwing/rejecting call stays `unknown`. Only `unsupported` changes anything, and only for `.mov` / `.m4v` sources — the Apple containers that are HEVC in practice for this library. `.mp4` is deliberately excluded (it carries H.264/VP9/AV1 just as often, and a wrong preempt costs a pointless ffmpeg encode, or a terminal "Video cannot be played" when ffmpeg is missing, on a file that would have played natively). The probe is never awaited before the first paint: until it resolves the surfaces use the original-bytes `src` exactly as before. PreviewPane and FullResViewer read the hint when the selected path changes; VideoTile reads it at click time (when its `<video>` is created). **Two-attempt contract (#787):** the error swap is now direction-aware — whichever source the element STARTED on, the first `error` swaps to the other one, and only the second error is terminal. A native start that fails swaps to the transcode (the long-standing behaviour); a hint-chosen transcode start that fails swaps back to the original bytes. That second direction is what makes the hint safe to be wrong: stock Chrome on Windows reports HEVC unsupported without the paid extension, so a preempted `.mov` whose transcode is unavailable would otherwise hit `HTTP 501` and go terminal even when its bytes are plain H.264 that the browser could have played. The contract still applies where ffmpeg genuinely is missing — a dev checkout without ffmpeg on PATH, or a bundle whose binaries were removed — even though the packaged release now ships its own (#854, below). Exactly one swap is allowed in either direction — it can never loop. **Why the passthrough still returns an MP4 (#853):** the endpoint could have handed back the source bytes instead, but that breaks the files it aims to help. `.avi` is a walked video extension (`scanner/media.py`) and no browser demuxes AVI, and Firefox does not demux QuickTime, so an H.264 `.mov` served raw fails there while today's encode works — and the two-attempt swap cannot rescue either case, because the source it swaps to is byte-identical to the one that just failed. Remuxing keeps the universally-playable MP4 container, needs no frontend change, and still deletes the encode. A stream copy that ffmpeg refuses falls through to the encode, so the passthrough can never leave a file less playable than it was before #853.
- **Related:** `infrastructure/transcode_service.py`; `app/web/routes/media.py`; [`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`](../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) (the bundled build's tag, checksum and LGPL text); `frontend/src/api/client.ts` (`mediaUrl` opts); `frontend/src/lib/videoCapabilities.ts` + [`videoCapabilities.test.ts`](../frontend/src/lib/videoCapabilities.test.ts); QA scenarios [`qa/web/scenarios/s69_video_playback.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s69_video_playback.py), [`qa/web/scenarios/s70_video_transcode_fallback.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s70_video_transcode_fallback.py), [`qa/web/scenarios/s71_grid_video_tiles.py`](../qa/web/scenarios/s71_grid_video_tiles.py) (all three use a VP9-in-MP4 fixture, so the container gate keeps them on the original-bytes path); integration test `tests/integration/test_transcode_integration.py`; unit test `tests/test_transcode_service.py`.
- **Last verified:** 2026-06-29 (V2 transcode fallback); 2026-07-03 (#737 — ffmpeg preset `fast`→`ultrafast` to cut the first-view transcode stall; FullResViewer now shows the same "Preparing video…" indicator as PreviewPane); 2026-09-05 (#787 — HEVC capability pre-check chooses the initial `src`; measured in headless Chromium: `hvc1`/`hev1` report `supported: false`, VP9 `supported: true`); 2026-09-06 (#853 — ffprobe codec passthrough; exercised through the real server on port 8772: an H.264+AAC `.mov` returned 200 `video/mp4` whose video stream MD5 matched the source's exactly, an HEVC `.mov` still came back re-encoded to `h264`, and an H.264 `.avi` came back as a real MP4; a 10-bit H.264 High 10 `.mov` took the ENCODE path and came back `h264` / `yuv420p`); 2026-09-07 (#854 — release bundles a pinned LGPL **shared** ffmpeg in `_internal\ffmpeg\`, verified through a real local PyInstaller build: SHA-256 matches the publisher's `checksums.sha256`; the built bundle carries the 10-file set once (no loose duplicates) and `ffmpeg -version` / `-encoders` / a throwaway `libopenh264` encode all pass from `dist\`; the frozen exe's own web smoke resolved `dist\photo-manager\_internal\ffmpeg\ffmpeg.exe` with a chocolatey ffmpeg still on PATH and logged `H.264 encoder for transcodes: libopenh264`; `GET /api/media?path=…&transcode=h264` against that bundle returned 200 `video/mp4`, 496 491 bytes in 0.49 s, probing as `h264` / `yuv420p` 1920x1080 + `aac`, from a 2 s 1080p HEVC source; the dev-checkout path still resolves the PATH ffmpeg and selects `libx264`. Sizes measured on that build: the staged set is 127.9 MiB (the static variant was 218.0 MiB), which is exactly the `dist\` delta between two builds differing only in the staging dir (292.2 → 420.1 MiB); the finished bundle is 426.4 MiB and its release zip 172.4 MiB, of which 53.9 MiB is ffmpeg — the same zip without `_internal\ffmpeg\` is 118.5 MiB); 2026-09-07 (#862 — first-audio-only verdict + shared ffprobe cap, exercised through the real server on port 8779: a `.mov` carrying H.264/`yuv420p` + AAC **+ a second PCM track** logged `Remuxed (no re-encode) dual_audio.mov` and came back 200 `video/mp4` in 0.15 s with exactly ONE audio stream (`aac`) — before #862 the same file logged `Transcoded`; an HEVC control still took the encode. For the cap, 6 concurrent cold requests for 6 distinct H.264 clips were served through a deliberately slow `ffprobe` shim (`ping -n 3`, then the real binary with the same argv) while a `tasklist` sampler counted live probes: peak 2, and the responses landed in three pairs at 2.22 s / 4.43 s / 6.67 s — the same six shim invocations fired with no semaphore in the path peak at 6 and finish together in 2.16 s)
